# Verification — 2026-09-08

## Workflows, static and dynamic: their own chats, one history, workflows calling workflows — 2026-10-19

Requested 2026-10-19: "I don't like the "missions" label, I would still prefer the dynamic vs static. Allow one workflow to call another workflow as well (get it's results, etc). Also have a sequential history with details, since some workflows may include loops and have execution get back to a certain point. Also can you make the chats created by the workflow isolated to the workflow? So they don't show on the active list, neither trigger notifications. Also I should not be able to interact with the chats directly. make the current running workflow show in the active list. I believe the current UX is a improvement over the previous version, but it still feels some clumbersome, the way thinks are organized is too much steps and not well bundled together, the graph itself looks good though. If it already doesn't do this, when creating new chats if the account load-balance is enabled already use it on sub-chats. I tested it and I noticed that the last step went without being marked as completed." The screenshot showed a completed dynamic workflow whose last task, Fix review findings, read Skipped with a stop mark. Its preview store showed why its workers were in Active: the orchestrator had spawned them with the old `listed` option.

- Names: the interface says static and dynamic (the chat's switch, headings, alerts, the orchestrator's instructions and `[Workflow update]` messages, which older `[Mission update]` messages still summarize as); the orchestrator's tools are `dynamic_*` and the general one `dynamic_start`. Saved runs keep `mission`.
- Isolation: every chat a run starts belongs to it: out of Active and History and the pending badge, no desktop or relay alert of its own (`isolated`, `runChats`; the relay quiets questions of run chats too), and read-only (`readOnly`: no composer, Rewind, Retry, Fork, Snooze or settings, Part of its workflow and Open workflow instead). What such a chat asks becomes the run's: `asking` on its agent, counted with the run's questions, one alert under the workflow (`watchQuestions`), answerable in the run. The Agent step's and `dynamic_spawn`'s `listed` option is gone, `listed` agents of older runs count as the run's, and a chat of yours an orchestrator writes to is marked `external`.
- Active list: runs you or a trigger started lead it while they run, with their elapsed time, question mark or pause mark (`listedRuns`).
- One view: the Graph and Runs tabs became a header with Run and a Runs picker; a run shows its graph beside (or, in the panel, above) one column: Waiting for you, its goal or inputs, result, time, where it runs (Change) and notes, and a numbered History (`staticTimeline`, `dynamicTimeline`) in which loop items and returns have entries of their own, each opening on its input, output, error, attempts, reply, chat or child run; choosing a card filters it. The tabs of the old run panel (Details, History, Notes, Chats) are gone.
- Workflows calling workflows: a Run workflow step runs a saved workflow with inputs or a dynamic one with a goal template and its orchestrator, and continues with its result as text and, when it parses, JSON (`{{key.json…}}`), naming the run in `{{key.run}}`; a retry after a failed child starts another. `dynamic_run_workflow` takes a goal too. Children name their `parent`.
- Balancing: with Start on the least used account on, sub-chats leave out the accounts Settings leaves out (unless none other can answer) and new Agent steps start on Least used. Sub-chats without an account already took the least used one.
- The last step: skipped tasks show a muted check and Skipped; when a dynamic workflow completes its plan settles (tasks whose agents all finished done, others cancelled), tasks still going stop spinning, and steps an ended run never reached or tasks it never began fade.
- Found while testing: a run this window drove showed its history only as of when its view opened, since the driver's own writes never read it back (`written`, and `load` now takes each entry once and keeps a newer copy); and a history sheet in the panel lay over the graph under the narrow rule for settings.
- Tests: 856 Vitest tests in 105 files, new ones for agent questions, dynamic and retried Run workflow steps, the static history with a return, the dynamic history's cards, skipped and unreached tasks, `dynamic_run_workflow`, run chats in the store and relay pushes; `tests/workflows.spec.ts` covers the Runs picker, the history and its filter, the Active row, a question answered in the run without a chat alert, and a read-only worker chat.
- Native: `scripts/workflows-native-smoke.mjs` with real Codex built and ran a static workflow (GREETING NATIVE), and a dynamic one planned, spawned a worker and completed with its task done, 7 history entries and a read-only worker chat. The QA build first refused core plugin calls (`app.identifier not allowed. Plugin not found`) from a stale build-script output in its target; rebuilding the app crate's build script fixed it.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,441 files), 856 Vitest tests, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, 530 Rust tests with 44 opt-in ignored (`--test-threads=8`), and all 435 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained, each shard whole.
- Not exercised: relay pushes against a released relay (unit-tested), a dynamic child started by a Run workflow step with real CLIs (unit- and mock-tested), the Viewer, and Claude as orchestrator in the QA app (its Claude login is signed out).
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: an agent's question stays answerable in its read-only chat as well as in the run; the chat you started a dynamic workflow in and a static workflow's assistant stay writable; runs another run started are not listed in Active; the history oldest first and following its end; a step's entries filtered by choosing its card; notes folded under the result; balancing off keeps Least used for steps without an account.

## A new workflow shows its graph beside its chat — 2026-10-20

Requested 2026-10-20: "On a new workflow it should already show the graph on the right always, keep the rule that if we open an already created static workflow it doesn't show the chat by default"

- A new workflow chat (the Workflow mode's +, its tab with nothing to return to) is laid out as a workflow from the start: its chat card on the left, open and without its ×, and on the right the graph the workflow starts with (`WorkflowDraft.svelte`, shown while `workflowDraft`): a static workflow's Manual trigger, or a dynamic one's orchestrator naming the computer and account it will run on from Runs on and the Agent picker, switching with the welcome's Static and Dynamic. The first message puts the workflow's own graph in its place, its canvas exactly where the new one's was (the draft's name takes the height of a saved workflow's name field). A saved workflow opened from the list still shows its graph with its chat closed.
- Browser: `tests/workflows.spec.ts` checks the new static workflow's Manual card beside the open chat without Close the chat, the switch to the orchestrator and back, the canvas staying in place when the workflow is made, the dynamic draft's Desktop · Claude CLI login line and its canvas staying in place too, and, as before, a saved workflow opening from the list with its chat closed. Screenshots of both new kinds, of each just made, and of a phone, where the chat covers the graph before and after the first message alike.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,443 files), 858 Vitest tests, the build, and all 445 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained; the second shard ran whole once more after `tests/notifications.spec.ts:376` missed its wait for a phone alert. No Rust changed.

## The title on the window's surface, the chat and its side panel two cards — 2026-10-20

Requested 2026-10-20: "Make the title be out of the right card, same for the top navbar buttons, also split the chat and the artifact/right sidebar like we had before (two separated cards)"

- The sidebar keeps the window's own surface without an edge, and the window's title and its buttons now lie on it too, without a line, at the window's top and right edges. Below them the chat is a card again (`.card-layout`, 12px from the window's sides and bottom), a docked artifact or sub-agent panel a second `--bg` card beside it with the 12px gap between them as its resize handle, and Settings and Connections one card (`.page-card`). The one big card of the round before, its line handle and the frame's drag margins are gone.

Screenshots of a new chat, a chat with a docked artifact (dark and light), Connections, a workflow and the phone.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,442 files), 858 Vitest tests, the build, and all 445 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained; the second shard ran whole twice more, after two timing budgets in `tests/performance.spec.ts` and a reload in `tests/reload-keeps-replies.spec.ts` (9 of 9 alone) ran late in a slow run, then after `tests/notifications.spec.ts:376` missed its wait for a phone alert while the sockets would not drain. No Rust changed.

## A Linear-style frame: the sidebar on the window, the rest one card — 2026-10-20

Requested 2026-10-20: "Now can you update the general layout for it to be more similar to linear app where the left sidebar is part of the entire part and the right part is a “card”" (with a screenshot of Linear).

- In a window wider than 760px the window's surface is the sidebar's (`--bg-sidebar`), the sidebar loses its edge, and `.main-area`, the window's title with whatever it shows, is one card: `--bg` inside a `--border` line with `--radius-xl` corners, 8px from the window's top, right and bottom. The frame's margins drag the window (`data-tauri-drag-region` on `.app-shell`). The chat's own card and the Settings and Connections card of the day before are gone; a chat's toolbar line still divides it from the conversation, and Settings and Connections keep the title bar's line. Phones keep the page edge to edge.
- Docked artifact and sub-agent panels are panes of the card behind their own `--border` line again, resized from an invisible 8px handle centred on that line (`PanelResize` with a `gutter` of 0, which takes no room). The workflow chat card stays a card of its own beside the graph.
- Found by the browser suite: a handle of no width read as hidden and missed drags 4px from the line, so it is 8px wide with -4px margins; `tests/window-scroll.spec.ts` measures the title bar from the card's top, and the sidebar test counts the card's margin from the window's edge in the 600px it keeps for the main area.
- Browser: `tests/chat-card.spec.ts` (Edge and WebKit) covers the frame (no sidebar edge, the card's corners, the title inside it, no card within it), a docked panel's line and its centred handle by keys and pointer, and Settings and Connections in the card. Screenshots of a new chat, a chat with a docked artifact (dark and light), Settings, Connections, a workflow and the phone.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,442 files), 858 Vitest tests, the build, and all 445 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained; the second shard ran whole again after `tests/notifications.spec.ts:376` missed its wait for a phone alert. No Rust changed.

## Agent and Workflow modes in the sidebar — 2026-10-20

Requested 2026-10-20: "Can you add a tab switch button so I can switch between agent and workflow mode?"

- The sidebar's **Agent** and **Workflow** tabs under the wordmark replace the Conversations header and the foldable Workflows row (`sidebarMode`, `.mode-switch`): Agent shows the chat tabs and lists with the pending chats badge, Workflow the list of workflows with the count of what runs. The mode follows what is on screen, and a tab returns to what its mode showed last (`places`, `returnTo`), else to a new chat or workflow chat. The + beside the tabs starts a chat or a workflow by mode; the phone's drawer stays open on the mode it switched to; the arrow keys switch and keep the focus on the tabs; a row whose chat belongs to the other mode hands its focus to that mode's tab. The status region that announces pending chats sits beside the tabs, outside the tab's name.
- Found while testing: four focus fallbacks looked for any selected tab in the sidebar and now found the Agent tab first; they look in the chat tabs (`.conversation-tabs`).
- Browser: `tests/workflows.spec.ts` covers the switch both ways with its list, search and new workflow, the arrow keys and Home, returning to a workflow as it was, Ctrl+N's Agent mode, a running workflow opened from the Active list by keyboard (its tab focused), the Active checks through Agent, and a phone drawer that stays open on the mode it switched to (Edge and WebKit); `tests/workspace.spec.ts` checks the two mode tabs above the chat tabs. Screenshots of both modes, dark and light, on the desktop and in the phone drawer.
- Native: `scripts/workflows-native-smoke.mjs`, which now switches modes for the chats and the workflows, built and ran a static workflow (GREETING NATIVE) and completed a dynamic one with its worker through real Codex in an isolated QA build.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,441 files), 858 Vitest tests, the build, and all 443 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained; the second shard ran whole again after two relay-backed phone tests failed under load (both passed alone, then with their shard). No Rust changed.

## Gap handles, a resizable workflow side panel and two chat card variants — 2026-10-19

- The gap between two panels is now the handle that resizes them, invisible but for its cursor and a keyboard focus line (`PanelResize` `gutter`): between a workflow's chat card and its graph, between the graph and the panel at its right (step settings, workflow settings and run history share one remembered width), and between an ordinary chat and a docked artifact or sub-agent panel (rendered by those panels before their dialog). Ordinary chats show either the conversation (`.chat-body`, a new wrapper of everything below the toolbar) or the toolbar as the card, chosen in Settings → Appearance → Chat card.
- Browser: `tests/chat-card.spec.ts` (Edge and WebKit) covers the default and switched card, the radio keys, the stored choice, and the side panel's gap handle by keys and pointer; `tests/workflows.spec.ts` covers the side panel's and the chat card's gap handles. The connection right-click now waits for the graph to stop gliding, which WebKit hit once the side panel's gutter changed the canvas width.
- Screenshots of both card variants, plain and with an artifact panel docked, in Edge at 1380px.

## Workflows: chat card everywhere, history on demand, a full-height workflow list — 2026-10-19

- Every workflow, dynamic ones included, is graph-first with its chat as a card closed until opened (open after creating or converting), now the whole chat with its toolbar (actions fold into … below 560px of chat, `chat-area` container) and a close button outside the fold, resizable from its right edge (`PanelResize` `side: 'right'`, share 0.36); the dynamic split panel is retired (`WorkflowPanel.svelte` deleted). A run's history is a column on demand (History, ×, card choice, a new wait), narrower beside a narrow graph and over it only below 560px. The sidebar's unfolded workflow list takes the chat lists' place. Ordinary chats are cards too (an experiment), with docked side panels as cards. Agent, Screen and Run workflow orchestrator choices take Same as workflow chat (`WORKFLOW_ACCOUNT`).
- Browser: `tests/workflows.spec.ts` (six tests, the menus one also in WebKit; the long first one split in two after it timed out once under a full run's load) covers the card's toolbar, close, keyboard and pointer resizing, the 36% share across window sizes for a dynamic workflow, the history toggle, its opening for an approval and closing, sub-chat titles in the window, the full-height list hiding the tabs, and the account choice saved as `workflow`; `workflow-engine.test.ts` resolves it (assistant, an orchestrator that started the run over the assistant, another agent's account falling back to the least used). Screenshots reviewed at 1380, 1900 and 720px, dark and light.
- Found on the way: the history opened for an approval lay over the graph in a medium-width view and hid the cards it filters, so it now keeps to a narrower column there; the card's close button folded into … with the actions until moved out of the fold; a docked artifact panel left the chat less than its 420px once the workspace had padding and a gap (side panels now measure the room without them, `panelRoom` in `src/lib/side-panels.ts`); and the chat area as a size container kept WebKit phones from scrolling a chat to its end after a send, so it is a container only above 760px.
- Native: `scripts/workflows-native-smoke.mjs`, which now folds the workflow list before looking for chats (an earlier run leaves it unfolded) and opens History before counting entries, passed with real Codex: a workflow built and run from a chat's tools (GREETING NATIVE), a dynamic workflow completed with 7 history entries and only the driver chat's process left, and the worker's chat read-only in the card with Orchestrator back. Its first screenshot after opening that chat caught the graph before it fit the narrower canvas, which takes a frame and a 200ms glide.

## Workflows page, top-down graphs and runs beside the chat — 2026-10-19

Requested 2026-10-19: "Can you update the workflows button, I don't like how it looks right. When resizing the window it should resize the chat / workflow division proportionally. I don't like the overall UX of the workflows, can you improve it?" A review of every workflows screen at 1920 and 1440 px found: the button read as a third input box; it switched the sidebar's list and opened a blank chat at once, leaving the tabs with nothing selected; graphs ran sideways in a tall panel, shown at half size and cut off; the panel's header held seven controls; a run replaced the chat as a page; and a mission page had two identical chat buttons.

- Sidebar: Workflows is a quiet row like a chat's, counting what runs, that opens the Workflows page; the sidebar's workflows list is gone (`WorkflowList.svelte` deleted), so the tabs always show their own lists, and the heading's + is always New conversation.
- Workflows page (`WorkflowsHome.svelte`): Needs you, Running, Your workflows as cards (description, triggers, steps, last run), Recent runs, a search and New, and an empty state with New workflow. Opening a run of a saved workflow opens that workflow's chat with the run in its panel.
- New workflow chats choose Workflow or Mission with a two-way switch under the heading instead of a Create picker in the composer, and every workflow chat is named by its workflow.
- Split: the docked panel keeps a share of the space (half at first, remembered per device) as the window resizes (`PanelResize`'s `share`); on narrow windows it waits behind the bar above the chat, which a drawer would cover.
- Graph: top to bottom (`src/lib/workflow-layout.ts`), branches in their ports' order, a card's error port at its side and shown once used or while selected, connections that skip rows routed around the steps between (`edgeDetour`, `WorkflowEdge.svelte`), graphs laid out sideways before laid out again on opening, and a graph too big to read whole shown from its top.
- Panel: Run, settings, expand and close in the header, a "to fix" count that opens the settings, Graph and Runs tabs, Add step and Arrange over the graph, and Delete inside the settings; a run shows in the workflow's place with Back to its runs, and Run starts at once when there is nothing to choose. A mission page keeps one chat button.
- Found while reviewing: a schedule an agent added through `workflow_save` had no start time, so a slot earlier that day started a run at once; new schedules now get the time they were saved and kept ones keep theirs.
- Tests: `workflow-layout.test.ts` (5) and `workflow-tools.test.ts` (1) are new, the mission update's format is checked in `workflow-mission.test.ts`, and `tests/workflows.spec.ts` covers the page, the switch, the chat's name, the even split through two window sizes, the top-down order, a run in the panel with its Runs list, the page's recent runs and a run opened from them.
- Native: `scripts/workflows-native-smoke.mjs` opens workflows from the Workflows page; its run with real Codex built and ran a workflow (GREETING NATIVE) and completed a mission, with only the driver chat's Codex process left. Screenshots: `artifacts/workflows-native/`.
- Pipeline, on this change over b963499 (another session's commit, which landed while it was verified): `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,441 files), 849 Vitest tests in 105 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, 530 Rust tests with 44 opt-in ignored (`--test-threads=8`), all 435 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained, each shard whole, and the native smoke again. Earlier runs of shard 2 failed timing tests (`performance.spec.ts:540` at 27.5 to 30 ms against 25, `:483`, once `private-workspaces.spec.ts:370`) while another session's render job used up to 19 cores; alone they passed (16 to 19 ms), and the timing spec run with four workers on an archive copy of HEAD failed the same tests as often as this change.
- Not exercised: the Workflows page in the Viewer against a released relay, and a Claude assistant in the app (the QA identity's Claude login is signed out).
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: a page of its own rather than a list in the sidebar; New opening the workflow chat, where the agent is chosen, rather than a box on the page; half and half to start; narrow windows keeping the graph behind the bar; error branches leaving from a card's side; a connection's detour on whichever side is shorter; Run without a dialog when nothing needs choosing.

## A reload of the window stops nothing — 2026-10-19

Reported 2026-10-19 with a screenshot of a reply ending in "This response was interrupted when the app closed." and an empty Active tab: "the app just refreshed and it stopped all my chats".

- Cause: the installed app (0.11.0) had run since 09:09:28 and its WebView2 renderer since 09:09:29, yet its WebView2 history holds a `RELOAD` visit at 09:28:52 that ended the 09:09 page (`page_end_reason` reload, 19 min 23 s), with no WebView2 failure, Windows error or diagnostics report: the page itself reloaded. Nothing in the desktop page reloads by itself (Reload window needs the stall banner, which leaves a report), while WebView2's browser keys (F5, Ctrl+R and their variants) and its right-click Reload were live. A preview build had opened next to the app at 09:26. Every page load then cancelled each running reply natively (`interrupt_for_reload`, the 2026-09-12 design), read them as interrupted and moved every chat to History, as an app start does: three replies stopped (09:18, 09:19 and 09:25).
- Fix: a page load only detaches each reply from its document (`Runs::reloaded`); the reply runs on and keeps its events, each at its first place and latest report (`src-tauri/src/reply_feed.rs`). The reloaded window lists the replies (`live_runs`) before it reads the workspace, leaves them running, and follows each with `follow_run` (`startReply`'s `resume`); relay requests are taken up again once the relay answers (`resumeJob`). `app_session` says `reloaded` to every later page of the process, so a reload leaves chats where they were. WebView2's reload and history keys and menu items are switched off for the window (`src-tauri/src/page_guard.rs`).
- Tests: 18 Rust tests (`reply_feed`, the reload cases in `runner.rs`, `page_guard`, `app_session`), five Vitest tests in `transport.test.ts` (a resumed relay request, one the relay no longer holds, one never taken up, `loadWorkspace` with live runs, `appSession`), and `tests/reload-keeps-replies.spec.ts` (a reply followed through a reload with its chat in Active, a stray run let go while the reply without a run reads as interrupted, and an app start still clearing Active).
- Native: `scripts/reload-native-smoke.mjs` (build with `scripts/native-reload.tauri.json`) drove real Codex (GPT-6.1-Sol) in an isolated build: a reply reloaded 3 s into `Start-Sleep -Seconds 25` stayed in Active, showed the running call after the reload and ended there with RELOAD COMMAND DONE; a reply waiting on `studio_ask_user` showed its question again after the reload and answered COLOR BLUE once Blue was sent; nothing was left in `live_runs` and the renderer logged no errors.
- Keys: keys sent through CDP never reach WebView2's accelerator handling (a throwaway build logging `AcceleratorKeyPressed` saw nothing for CDP's Escape, F5 and Ctrl+R) yet reload the page, so they cannot test the guard. Posted `WM_KEYDOWN` F5 messages reached the guard (logged as key 0x74), and both handlers registered, but a window that is not in the foreground reloads for neither the guarded nor an unguarded control build, and taking the foreground would have taken it from the user's desktop: F5 and the menu on a focused window were not exercised.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,437 files), 843 Vitest tests in 103 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, 530 Rust tests with 44 opt-in ignored (`--test-threads=8`), and all 435 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained. The first runs of shards 2 and 3 lost seven tests while a Java process and another chat's Rust build loaded the machine (the 300-message sub-agent update at 26.1 ms against 25, and 5-second waits for startup detection and for the workflow graph's first load); each passed alone, and both shards then passed whole.
- Not exercised: a relay request resumed through the production relay, macOS and Linux, whose webviews keep their own context menus (a reload there now only detaches replies too).

## Workflows from a chat, with triggers, Confirm and chosen computers — 2026-10-17

Requested 2026-10-17, after trying the first version: "Make the workflows button be above the active / history / screens. Also allow for many starts (many triggers), not just schedules and manual ones. Also I don't like workflow + mission buttons, and for static workflows I should have a way to directly update the workflow talking with an AI agent there like a normal chat. For runs on, I should be able to pick VPS, and for continue on another computer, I should be able to pick which PCs. Default agent should be claude, and default reasoning should be max, save the latest picked values. A new mission will work more like a chat as well. I should be able to create a new node (step) from dragging the arrow from the end of the other nodes, add a confirm step. Also some right side have unwanted horizontal scroll. Add a doted background to the graph. Change the arrange icon".

- Sidebar: a Workflows button above the Active, History and Screens tabs, counting what is in progress, replaces the tab, and the pending-chat badge stays beside Conversations.
- Chats: the button opens a workflow chat whose composer chooses Workflow or Mission; its first message creates a workflow with the chat as its assistant or a mission with the chat as its orchestrator (`bindWorkflowChat`), New workflow and New mission are gone (`MissionDialog.svelte` deleted), and the graph shows beside the chat (`WorkflowPanel.svelte`; its default width leaves the chat 640px, and the toolbar's names show from 520px). Workflow chats start with Claude at its maximum reasoning, then the choices made last there (`workflow-preferences.ts`). A mission update lists its events one a line before the agents' replies, so its summary in the chat names the first event without the chat id and counts the rest.
- Graph: triggers of seven kinds on as many Trigger steps as a workflow needs (`workflow-triggers.ts`, the relay's webhook route), the Confirm step, a step added by dragging a connection to empty space, a dotted background, Arrange with a wand and its name, step settings that never scroll sideways, edits saved by themselves and merged with the assistant's (`mergeDefinition`), and a graph that shows its start at 65% when it is too wide to read whole, in the panel as on the page.
- Computers: Runs on lists every computer a run can be driven by, a server host included, and Continue on chooses which may take over (`DriverPicker.svelte`, `mayContinue`), for a run while it runs and for a workflow's triggered runs.
- Tests: 57 Vitest tests in eight `workflow-*.test.ts` files (triggers new; webhooks in the relay tests; Confirm, Continue on, chat-created missions and the update's format in the engine and mission tests), and `tests/workflows.spec.ts` rewritten: a workflow started from a chat, built by its assistant beside its graph (Claude, max), with a Confirm step dragged out of a connection, saved by itself and an inspector without sideways scroll; a run through Confirm from the graph; and a mission whose orchestrator is the chat, plans, spawns, is refused a foreign call and hears its agent in an update summarised without its id. `workspace.spec.ts` checks the button in place of the tab.
- Native: `scripts/workflows-native-smoke.mjs` opens workflows through the button now; two runs with real Codex (the second after the update's new format) built and ran a workflow from a chat's tools (GREETING NATIVE) and completed a mission whose worker answered WORKER DONE, with only the driver chat's process left. Screenshots: `artifacts/workflows-native/`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,437 files), 836 Vitest tests in 103 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, 509 Rust tests with 42 opt-in ignored (`--test-threads=8`), and all 431 browser tests in three shards with the sockets drained. The first run of shard 1 lost two tests while Dota 2 used four cores and Vite was cold (a page whose 74 module requests never answered, and a Viewer reply that missed its 5-second wait); each passed alone, three times for the Viewer test, and the shard then passed whole, as did the final run of every shard on the committed code.
- Not exercised: a webhook through the production relay (it needs a release with the route), a run driven by the VPS (the preview is not paired), chat, workflow, computer and usage triggers firing in a real app, and a Claude assistant or orchestrator in the app (the QA identity's Claude login is signed out).
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: seven trigger kinds; a webhook's secret in its address, checked before its body is read; triggers watched by the computer a workflow runs on, firing only on what happens after watching began; Continue on defaulting to every other computer; the chat keeping 640px beside a new panel; Workflow or Mission remembered per device; a run of a static workflow opening as a page rather than beside a chat.

## Workflows: static graphs and agent-run missions — 2026-10-17

Requested 2026-10-16: "Can you implement a complete "workflow" feature? This will allow two workflows modes: static and dynamic, the static can be created by agents and humans and edited by both as well, and dynamic is managed only the agent. … I want to see everything that is being done in the same 2D graph live, and also a history of what happened". The design is docs/WORKFLOWS.md.

- Static workflows: a graph editor (`WorkflowView.svelte`, `WorkflowCanvas.svelte` on `@xyflow/svelte`, loaded with the first workflow opened) with sixteen kinds of steps, their settings beside the graph (`WorkflowInspector.svelte`), schedules, Run with start inputs (`WorkflowStart.svelte`), and an engine that runs them as tokens with templates, loops, merges, error ports, retries, waits for usage resets, sign-in, offline computers and interrupted replies (`src/lib/workflow-engine.ts`).
- Missions: New mission (`MissionDialog.svelte`) or `mission_start` starts an orchestrator chat with the mission brief and thirteen mission tools; the engine wakes it with what happened, reminds it at growing intervals, retries it, switches its account at a limit and briefs a new session on another computer when the driver changes (`src/lib/workflow-mission.ts`). The graph shows the orchestrator, the plan's tasks with their dependencies, each agent with what it does now, questions and runs it started (`src/lib/workflow-view.ts`); the run panel has Details, History, Notes and Chats, and answers questions inline.
- Store and drivers: definitions and runs live in the workflow store (`src/lib/workflow-store.ts`) on the relay (`relay/workflows.ts`) or, until a relay has it, in app data `workflows/` (`src-tauri/src/workflow_files.rs`); one desktop drives each run under a lease and hands it over (`src/lib/workflow-client.svelte.ts`), conflicting writes merge per item (`src/lib/workflow-merge.ts`), and a removed id is never accepted again, so a deleted scheduled run does not return.
- Agent tools: every saved Claude and Codex chat with tools gets six workflow tools and an orchestrator the mission tools, answered by the executing computer's window (`src-tauri/src/providers/workflow_calls.rs`, `answer_workflow_call`, `pending_workflow_calls`; `RunRequest.mission` is part of the parked-process identity).
- Sidebar: a Workflows tab after Screens, with icons in place of the tab names when the sidebar is narrow; workflow chats stay out of Active, History, the pending badge and chat alerts, and show a Workflow banner above them. Alerts: questions, Notify steps and a run's end, once per desktop and through relay pushes.
- Tests: 45 Vitest tests in seven `workflow-*.test.ts` files (store, model, templates, merge, relay routes and pushes, engine, missions), the relay's revision answer in `relay.test.ts`, six Rust tests in `workflow_calls.rs` and `workflow_files.rs` plus `codex_chat.rs`'s tool list, and `tests/workflows.spec.ts`: a workflow built in the editor and run, its chat kept out of the chat lists with its banner, and a mission whose orchestrator is briefed, plans, spawns, is refused a foreign call, hears its agent's reply and completes. `workspace.spec.ts`'s tab keys include Workflows.
- Real CLIs: the opt-in `installed_claude_answers_workflow_tools_through_the_window` (Claude Code 2.1.302, 4.7 s) and `installed_codex_answers_workflow_tools_through_the_window` (Codex 0.163.0, 13 s) called `workflow_list` and `mission_state`, which reached a stand-in window with the mission binding, and named both answers.
- Native: `scripts/workflows-native-smoke.mjs` (build with `scripts/native-workflows.tauri.json`) drives an isolated build with real Codex replies: a chat saved a three-step workflow with `workflow_save` and ran it with `workflow_run`; its Agent step started a chat of its own, kept out of the chat lists, that answered GREETING NATIVE; `workflow_status` read the run back; and `mission_start` started a mission whose orchestrator planned a task, spawned a worker that answered WORKER DONE, heard it and completed the mission, live in the graph. The first run found that a finished run still listed its step's and orchestrator's chats as running, and that the orchestrator's CLI process stayed parked after the reply that completed its mission; both are fixed (`settleStep`, `endSession`, `releaseAfterReply`), and the second run (81 s) asserts every agent done and only the driver chat's Codex process left. Screenshots: `artifacts/workflows-native/`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,432 files), 824 Vitest tests in 102 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, 509 Rust tests with 42 opt-in ignored (an earlier run at 32 threads failed `screens::run`'s timing test, as on HEAD, and passed with `--test-threads=8`), and all 430 browser tests in three shards with the local sockets drained.
- Not exercised: a released relay with the store (the relay of 0.11.0 has none, so until a release each desktop keeps its own workflows and the Viewer shows none), a handover between two real computers or to the VPS, a Claude orchestrator in the app (the terminal Claude login is signed out), screens steps, schedules firing in real time, WSL and macOS.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: workflow chats hidden from the chat lists unless an Agent step lists them; a sidebar tab rather than a page of its own; no slash command; runs driven by the computer that started them, continuing elsewhere by default; a reminder when a mission has nothing in progress (1, 5, 15, 30, then every 60 minutes) and an alert at the third; a missed schedule slot started late only within a day; the last 20 alerts kept in a run for delivery (its history keeps everything); the graph shown at 65% when it does not fit, from its start.

## Fast mode beside the composer, for Claude and Codex — 2026-10-16

Requested 2026-10-16: "Can you add the option for fast mode in claude and codex? If it already exists I just cant find it on the UI". Claude's Fast mode existed only as a picker inside Chat instructions and `/fast`; Codex had none.

- **Fast** beside the composer's Build/Plan picker (`fast-toggle` in `+page.svelte` and `styles.css`), shown while the selected Claude or Codex model offers Fast mode by its catalog entry. The chat keeps its choice when another model is selected; the toggle sets an explicit on or off, its hover says what the CLI says Fast mode gives, and it changes while a reply runs for the next message, named in the Next message hint (`fastModeName` in `src/lib/replies.ts`), with Switched to Fast mode or Standard speed on the reply that changes it. Chat instructions no longer holds it; fallback models and the thinking budget stay there.
- `/fast` turns it on or off for Claude and Codex, `/fast on`, `/fast off` and `/fast default` set it, also while a reply runs (`commandFastMode`), and a model without Fast mode refuses `/fast on`.
- Catalog (`src-tauri/src/models.rs`): Claude's alias query reads `supportsFastMode` from the CLI's `initialize` models, by value or resolved model, and opts in with `fastMode` in its `--settings` so the CLI says why the account cannot use it; `free`, `preference` and `extra_usage_disabled` travel as `fastModeBlocked`, shown on hover in Claude's words with the warning colour while on. Codex models offer it with a `priority` or Fast service tier (`fast` among older CLIs' extra speeds) and pass the tier's description.
- Codex (`codex_chat.rs`): `serviceTierForTurn` `fast` or `default` with each `turn/start`, never the thread's `serviceTier`, and outside the parked-process identity (`pool.rs`). `RunRequest::validate` takes Fast mode for Claude and Codex and keeps fallback models Claude's.
- Tests: `models.rs` (Codex tiers, Claude aliases matched by value and resolved model, lasting reasons only), `codex_chat.rs` (the turn's tier and never the thread's), `pool.rs` (Codex identity unchanged, Claude's changed), `providers.rs` (validation by provider), `replies.test.ts` (notices) and `commands.test.ts`; `tests/claude-fast-fallback.spec.ts` covers the toggle for both agents, models without it, the blocked state, changes and `/fast` while a reply runs, History state, validation, reload and a phone. `scripts/fast-fallback-native-smoke.mjs` drives the toggle.
- Real CLIs: the opt-in `installed_claude_names_each_alias_for_its_model` (Claude Code 2.1.301) found Fast mode for the CLI default and Opus 5.5 only, and `extra_usage_disabled` for that profile's account; `installed_codex_answers_with_fast_mode_on_and_off` (Codex 0.163.0, 13.7 s) completed a turn at each tier through the production loop. A probe showed Codex answering `thread/start` with `fast` as `priority`, and a tier a model does not offer as omitted with a `warning` notification.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,279 files), 779 Vitest tests in 95 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, the Rust tests (`screens::run`'s timing test failed at 32 threads as on HEAD, and all 502 passed with `--test-threads=8`), and all 428 browser tests in three shards with the local sockets drained, each passing whole, beside another session's Unreal and render jobs.
- Not exercised: the native app with a real reply (the smoke needs an isolated QA build and a signed-in CLI login), an account that can use Claude's Fast mode, and whether a reply was actually served faster: neither CLI reports it per reply. Hosts on 0.10.0 report no Fast mode in their catalogs, so the toggle stays hidden for chats they run until a release.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: beside the composer rather than in the toolbar; hidden for models without Fast mode while the chat keeps its choice; per conversation, with new chats starting without it; changeable while a reply runs; the warning colour rather than disabling the toggle when Claude says the account cannot use it; `/fast` toggling instead of opening a dialog.

## Four compared files take two rows of two — 2026-10-16

Requested 2026-10-16, after the comparison below: "Can you allow up to 4 files comparison?" A comparison already took any number of files, but four stood in one row of panes a quarter of the reply's width, about 190 pixels each.

- `chatColumns` in `src/lib/comparison.ts`: up to three panes in one row, four in two rows of two, rows of three past that, and two to a row where the comparison is narrower than 520 pixels (a container query on `.compare-view`). No maximum was added, following the rule against limits; a fifth file starts another row.
- The rows share the window's height (`ComparePane.svelte`: each pane at most `(100dvh - 380px) / rows`, between 140 pixels and a single row's `min(520px, 65vh)`), so a two-by-two grid of square model panes fits between the toolbar and the composer of a 900-pixel window. WebKit narrowed a pane whose height was capped to keep its shape (347 of 386 pixels); the pane now keeps its column's width in both engines.
- Tests: `comparison.test.ts` covers the columns and rows. `tests/compare-files.spec.ts` (Edge and WebKit) checks four images of four sizes in two rows of two at half the comparison's width, all four zoomed to one region by a double click on one, two by two in the file viewer, and two by two on a phone. Screenshots of three, four and five images and models, inline, expanded and on a phone, reviewed by eye.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,279 files), 778 Vitest tests in 95 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check` (no Rust change, so Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun), and all 427 browser tests in three shards with the local sockets drained, each passing whole; the comparison spec then passed 60 times in a row under four workers.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: two rows of two rather than four in a row; rows of three past four; the height shared between rows.

## Files compared side by side — 2026-10-16

Requested 2026-10-16: "Can you implement a variation of the show image/3d model that have a side by side comparison? It has two modes: free and synced (where both have the same rotation and position)".

- A new tool, compare_files, beside send_files (`src-tauri/src/providers/sent_files.rs`; Claude's agent_studio server and Codex's dynamic tools): `id`, two or more `files`, optional `labels`, one per file, and `caption`. It shares send_files' registered-call rules, path checks, result store, markers and ids, and records a sent-files group with `compare` and each file's `label`. A call left with fewer than two files that can be shown fails with each file's reason, and a label stays with its file past rejected ones. Its guidance follows send_files' in the prompt of every chat with Agent Studio's tools, and Work history names the calls Compare files and Send files.
- `CompareView.svelte` shows the panes in one row (`ComparePane`, `CompareImage`, `CompareModel`), each named by its label or file name with its size, in one shape (`paneAspect`), with Free and Synced, play and pause for every animated model, Lighting, zoom, Reset views and Expand comparison. `PaneSync` (`src/lib/comparison.ts`) passes a reader's move of one pane to the panes of its kind in the same place while synced, and syncs again from the pane moved last; the bar's zoom and reset act on one pane per kind then, since zooming both made the image that followed zoom twice.
- Models: `ModelView.svelte`'s per-model logic moved into `ModelPane` (`src/lib/model-pane.svelte.ts`) and its stage into `ModelStage.svelte`, so a reply's model and a comparison's models share loading, the views drawn for another device, animation and scenes per place. `createModelScene` reports the reader's camera moves (`onView`) and takes another scene's view (`setView`), spending its own glide first; scenes being built or following report nothing. A follower ended 193 edge pixels off the leader (up to 44 levels) after a drag: OrbitControls reports no move below its threshold, so a glide comes to rest a little past the view it last reported. The resting view is now reported once more, and the two panes match pixel for pixel.
- Images: `imageZoom(fill)` (`src/lib/image-zoom.ts`) lets a comparison's images fill their panes however small, so a 64-pixel texture and its upscale show at one size, and `shareView`/`takeView` pass a view as shares of the image. `ZoomableImage` takes `pane`: no bar of its own, keys only while focused, and in the chat the plain wheel and vertical swipes left to the conversation until it is zoomed in. A picture that arrives after the one beside it was zoomed, as one read from another computer can, takes the view of the pane moved last when it is first drawn (`PaneSync.shared`).
- The file viewer shows a comparison as one of the chat's files (`ViewedComparison`), its panes side by side, or one above another on a screen taller than wide (a container query), with the chat's mode.
- Tests: `src/lib/comparison.test.ts` (following by kind and place, syncing again from the pane moved last, which panes the buttons act on, the view a late pane takes, panes joining and leaving, pane shapes, viewer columns and names), `image-zoom.test.ts` (fill mode and a region shared between resolutions), `sent-files.test.ts` (the schema, labels, storage, a merge and markers that never join a comparison to a gallery), `tool-presentation.test.ts`, and Rust tests for parsing, labels past a rejected file, the two-file minimum, registration per tool for Claude and Codex, and the prompt. `tests/compare-files.spec.ts` (Edge, and WebKit for the image scenarios) checks the images' shared region through double click, drag, keys, the bar and Free; two copies of one model drawing identical pictures after synced turns, moves and zoom and different ones when free; the expanded view, its wheel, the mode's arrow keys and focus back on Expand; the chat's scenes given up meanwhile; a mixed comparison without a mode; side by side on a phone; and a picture held back until the other was zoomed, which arrives at the same region (it failed with the catch-up removed). They passed 80 times in a row under four workers. `tests/federation.spec.ts` adds two compared models a relay could not carry whole, whose views turn together while synced; `tests/desktop-helper.ts` can hold back chosen tool output reads.
- Real CLIs: the opt-in `installed_claude_compares_two_files_side_by_side` (7.7 s) and `installed_codex_compares_two_files_side_by_side` (15.6 s) passed: each called compare_files with both files and their labels and placed the marker.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,279 files), 777 Vitest tests in 95 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, and all 425 browser tests in three shards with the local sockets drained; shard 2 first failed `notifications.spec.ts:376`, which passed three times alone, and then passed whole. The comparison scenario's tolerance of six pixels came after that run, so the spec alone ran 80 more times. `cargo test` with eight test threads: 499 passed and 39 opt-in ignored. At the default 32 threads `screens::run::tests::an_action_past_its_time_is_stopped_with_what_it_printed` (a PowerShell action must print within its 2-second limit while hundreds of tests start processes) failed with these changes stashed as well, and it passed alone and with the other Screens tests.
- Not exercised: the native desktop app with a real reply (the CLI tests drive the production runner), a real touch screen and the Viewer on a phone. Until a release updates the relay, a development build paired with the production relay loses `compare` and `label` from synced replies after one sync, which then show as ordinary groups.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: a tool of its own rather than an option of send_files; Synced as the starting mode, held per comparison in the window's memory; images filling their panes; panes in one row, wrapping below 120 pixels; square panes when a model is shown; one play and pause for every model.

## The Reasoning dropdown opens an animated slider — 2026-10-15

Requested 2026-10-15, after trying the inline slider below in a preview build: "Can you make still a dropdown, where the slider only shows when opened? Also can you make the slider animated?"

- `ChoicePicker.svelte` has a `slider` mode. Its trigger is the dropdown's (brain icon, level, chevron) with `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, and opening it shows `ChoiceSlider.svelte` instead of a list, with focus on the slider. The keys step through the levels, each step applying at once and showing on the trigger; Escape and Enter close it and return focus to the trigger, as does the trigger itself (its press keeps focus in the slider until its click closes it), and a click elsewhere or focus leaving the picker closes it. The popover widens with the stops (`sliderWidth`: 288px up to five, 358px for seven). The toolbar's Reasoning is the dropdown again, `/reasoning` opens it, and the inline slider's narrow-window rules are gone.
- `ChoiceSlider.svelte` draws the slider itself above a native range input left invisible over the track, which keeps the keys and what assistive technology reads: a track with a dot at each stop, a fill and a thumb in `--accent-strong`, and each stop's name under it, the chosen one brighter. The thumb and fill move by transforms that glide over `--duration-slow` with `--ease-out`. A pointer pressed on a stop or a name chooses it, and a drag holds the thumb under the pointer, without a transition, and lets it glide to the nearest stop on release. The page handles every pointer itself, so on iOS a finger works anywhere on the track.
- Tests: `tests/reasoning-slider.spec.ts`, in Edge and WebKit, opens the slider from the mouse and the keyboard, steps it, closes it with Escape, Enter, the trigger and a click elsewhere, clicks a name and a point on the track, drags with the thumb held under the pointer until it settles on the nearest stop, and checks the request; the runner and the fill run a transform transition after a key, none while dragged, one after release and none under reduced motion; `/reasoning`; seven levels whose names stay inside the popover and apart, in light; and on a phone, taps on a stop and a name and a finger dragged through untrusted pointer events. The specs and native smoke scripts converted for the inline slider are back to their dropdown steps, with `chooseReasoning` (`tests/reasoning-helper.ts`) where they picked a level from the list. Reviewed the glide frame by frame (transitions paused at 0 to 400 ms) and screenshots in both themes and on a phone.
- Native: an isolated release build (`com.vinicius.agentstudio.slider-preview`, own data, no relay, automatic CLI updates off) opened the slider with GPT-6.1-Sol's seven levels, focused, ran both glides on a real key press, closed on Escape and drew as the browser builds do.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,269 files), 763 Vitest tests in 94 files, the build, and all 415 browser tests in three shards, the local sockets drained to about 400 while another program's local server held the rest. No Rust change.
- Not exercised: an iPhone.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: the slider stays open after each choice; the names under the stops; the lime accent for the fill and thumb, where the inline slider stayed neutral; the 260 ms glide.

## Reasoning is a slider — 2026-10-15

Requested 2026-10-15: "Can you make reasoning a slider?"

- `src/lib/components/ChoiceSlider.svelte`: a native range input, so the keyboard, screen readers and touch work as on any slider, drawn as a line with a dot at each stop, the line behind the thumb filled, a 12px thumb, and the chosen name after the track in the width of the longest, so nothing beside it moves while it changes. A finger or pen anywhere on the track chooses the stop under it and follows a drag, since iOS moves a range input only from its thumb; the mouse keeps the input's own handling. A value the page keeps puts the thumb back, and a value set before a larger maximum is set again once both are.

Phones give it a 96px track and a 44px height in the scrolling toolbar.

The finger sends untrusted pointer events, which the input itself ignores, so that part fails without the handler; the taps pass either way in both engines, since neither Playwright engine reproduces iOS. `src/lib/models.test.ts` covers the scale. The Reasoning steps of `workspace.spec.ts`, `remembered-agent.spec.ts` and `take-over.spec.ts` use `tests/reasoning-helper.ts`, as do the native smoke scripts driven through Playwright; the two driven through raw CDP set the input as its input event does. ## The Viewer's copy is a cache of the relay — 2026-10-15

Requested 2026-10-15, after the fix below: "the mobile just can't hold any data, it's just a viewer, how can it have a conflict?" It holds a copy of every chat and can change them, but every conflict copy a phone made came from data it only received: its saved copy and its saved sync baseline are written apart, and whatever the copy held beyond the baseline counted as the phone's own edit. Yes to making the copy a cache.

- `src/lib/change-marks.ts`: `pending()` names what changed here that the relay may not hold yet (what waits for a sync and what a sync under way took), `settleUnsynced()` lets a sync hand that back when it ends, and `resume()` starts a reloaded page from what its copy saved instead of counting every conversation as changed.
- `src/lib/browser-workspace.ts`: the Viewer's index carries `unpublished` (those conversations and whether the settings changed), written in the same IndexedDB transaction as the conversations; an index without it, as earlier releases wrote, counts every conversation as changed, as before. `replaceBrowserWorkspace` resumes the page's marks from it, and a downloaded copy starts with nothing unpublished.
- `src/lib/sync.ts` and `syncIncremental`: on the Viewer, a conversation not pending is `received`: the relay's copy stands, with a later step of a reply the phone follows itself (`sameRun` with its own copy as the base), and it goes once the relay no longer has it; the settings follow the relay when the phone changed none. A conversation the phone holds that neither the relay nor its baseline has, and that it did not make, is now part of the sync, so it leaves instead of lingering. A computer merges as before; the second merge over changes made while a sync ran never takes the rule.
- Tests: `src/lib/change-marks.test.ts` (pending through a sync that publishes, one that fails, one that took nothing, and a reload), `src/lib/browser-workspace.test.ts` (the record written with its conversation, an earlier index, a damaged record), `src/lib/sync.test.ts` (a rewind and two deletions elsewhere against a phone without its baseline, a followed reply a step ahead, the phone's own chat kept, and a chat the phone changed still merged as before), `src/lib/browser-transport.test.ts` (a downloaded copy starts with nothing unpublished). In `tests/viewer-sync-conflicts.spec.ts` a reopened Viewer without its baseline met a chat rewound and one deleted on the computer: the previous build left "Rewound elsewhere (conflict copy)" and put the deleted chat back on the relay; this one leaves neither. Another blocks the phone's writes, moves a chat to History there, closes the Viewer and lets the computer answer in that chat: reopened, the phone publishes its move with the computer's answer kept, and its record empties.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,265 files), 759 Vitest tests in 93 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, and all 407 browser tests in three shards with the local sockets drained. Shard 2 first failed `notifications.spec.ts:376` (a raw test push the worker did not show within 5 s): it failed 1 of 10 runs alone with this change and 3 of 10 on the previous commit, and the rerun shard passed whole.
- Not exercised: a phone itself.

## A chat no longer stays stuck on a reply nothing runs — 2026-10-15

Requested 2026-10-15: "a chat is stuck, with 2/4 plan, but nothing seems to be running, and I cant steer or send message to it."

- Cause, from the user's workspace and relay: the stuck chat and one other were "(conflict copy)" twins, written to the relay together (revision 205375, between 15:10 and 15:28 UTC) after the user had moved both originals to History on the desktop (15:02 and 15:10). Each copy held its reply at an earlier step, saved as running (plan 2 of 4), with the recorded work the relay puts back into a light upload (reasoning first, no answer block), so the phone's Viewer made them. Its saved copy held both chats as of about 14:40, while its saved baseline predated both (one was created at 12:56): the Viewer saves what a sync takes in before the baseline that came with it, and the phone closed between the two, or its IndexedDB write failed in the background. Reopened, it merged them without a baseline, and `sameRun` refused a one-sided move to History it could not attribute, so each chat became a copy of the phone's side. Of the chats that window touched, only those moved to History meanwhile were copied.
- Why it stayed stuck: a copy takes a new id while the run reports to the original's, so nothing ended the copy's reply. Stop on the desktop that runs its account called `cancel_run` for a run it no longer had, which does nothing, steering answered "This reply has ended. Your steering was not sent." and a queued message waited for the reply. Only a restart of the desktop app, which marks every reply saved as running interrupted, ended one.
- Fix: without a baseline, `sameRun` takes a copy that holds nothing the other lacks as the earlier one (`earlierCopy`), so the other's archive and settings stand. `conflictCopy` ends a running reply in any copy it makes ("This response went on in the original chat."). The window of the computer that runs a reply's account looks every ten seconds for replies saved as running that it holds no run for (its own, those of replies a queued message took over included, and relay jobs it executes) and ends them ("This response is no longer running.") once checks 20 seconds apart find no queued or running relay request for them, since another device publishes a chat before it sends the request; Stop ends one at once. Neither ending alerts anyone (`endedWithoutRun` in the desktop trackers and the relay's pushes).
- Tests: `src/lib/sync.test.ts` merges the user's case both ways without a baseline, still keeps two chats when the phone's side has a message of its own, and checks that copies from a settings conflict and from a rewind end their replies; 'preserves archive and restore changes' now expects the archive of the copy further along instead of two chats. All three failed on the previous `sync.ts`. `src/lib/transport.test.ts` covers `abandonedReplies`: the relay connection, the 20 seconds, a request still queued, Stop, another computer's reply and a live run. `tests/viewer-sync-conflicts.spec.ts` closes a Viewer, removes a chat from its saved baseline in IndexedDB, has the computer finish the reply, answer another message and move the chat to History, then reopens it: the previous build left "Install Chunky (conflict copy)" beside the original, this one takes the finished chat into History. `tests/abandoned-replies.spec.ts` pairs a desktop with a real relay holding two copies with running replies: Stop ends one at once, the other ends by itself after the second check with its queued message back in the composer, and the relay holds both as cancelled.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,265 files), 756 Vitest tests in 93 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check` (no Rust change, so Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun), and all 405 browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained.
- Not exercised: the phone itself and the native app. The Viewer and the relay's push change arrive with the next release.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: checks every ten seconds, the 20 seconds, the two texts, and an abandoned reply ending as cancelled rather than failed.

## Another account can be chosen while a reply runs — 2026-10-15

Requested 2026-10-15: "Can you allow switch the account while the agent still answering? In practice it will just switch on the next message, but I'll not need to wait for it to finish".

- The Agent picker of an existing Claude or Codex chat no longer locks while a reply runs: its `disabled`, `selectAgent` and `changeSettings` in `+page.svelte` dropped the running check, and the remaining guard still admits only another account of the chat's agent on its computer. A reply keeps the settings it started with, so the running one finishes on its account, and its steering, answers, Stop, sub-agents and tool results still go by the reply's own connection. The next message, a queued one included, takes the conversation's settings when it is sent, so it goes to the account chosen with `accountSwitch` and the transcript transfer of any switch between replies.
- The next-message hint names the account while a reply runs too (`accountSwitchPending` no longer waits for the reply), and its model and reasoning part compares the running reply's settings without the account (`nextReplyChanged`), so both show together: "Next message: Second Claude account · Opus · High reasoning", with the account's dot. Without that, an account chosen during a reply read as a model and reasoning change.
- A message for another account never takes over a Claude reply that only waits for background work, since `sameProcess` compares the connection: it waits for the reply to end, and the composer's button reads Queue message as soon as the account changes. An account chosen on one device while another computer runs the reply merges with that reply's checkpoints as a one-sided next-reply settings edit (`sameRun`).
- Tests: `tests/workspace.spec.ts` switches during a reply (hint alone and with model and reasoning, back to the reply's own account), queues a message that then goes to the new account with `accountSwitch`, and checks each reply's saved connection. `tests/take-over.spec.ts` checks that a waiting reply is not taken over across accounts. In `tests/federation.spec.ts` the device that started a reply running on the MacBook chooses another account meanwhile: the MacBook's window shows it, and after Stop both keep it, with the stopped reply on its own account; that test used the locked Agent picker as its sign of a running reply and now uses Chat instructions. `src/lib/sync.test.ts` merges such a switch both ways, and `src/lib/queue.test.ts` covers the take-over target. The three new browser scenarios passed four times in a row under four workers.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,264 files), 753 Vitest tests in 93 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check` (no Rust change, so Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun), and all 391 Edge and 12 WebKit browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained, each shard passing whole on the final version. Earlier runs, while another project's Vite dev server used three cores, failed `notifications.spec.ts:376` and `performance.spec.ts:540` (26.6 ms against 25) once each in shard 2, neither of which switches accounts: both passed alone, and `notifications.spec.ts:376` failed 2 of 10 runs with these changes stashed against 4 of 10 with them.
- Not exercised: the native desktop app with real CLIs, and the Viewer on a phone, which draws the same page.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: messages queued before the switch go to the new account too, as they already take a model or reasoning chosen meanwhile; the account comes first in the combined hint.

## A 3D model can be panned — 2026-10-14

Requested 2026-10-14: "Allow to pan a 3d model not just rotate".

- The viewer's OrbitControls had panning turned off. It now pans with the right button, Shift (or Ctrl or Cmd) with the left button, and two fingers, inline and expanded. In the chat a two-finger swipe that starts sideways pans the model while vertical swipes keep scrolling past it (`touch-action: pan-y` as before). A focused model pans with Shift and the arrow keys, a tenth of the view per press, the way the arrow keys move a zoomed image (Shift and Right shows more of what lies to the right; `sceneKey` in `src/lib/model-viewer.ts`, `pan` in `src/lib/model-scene.ts`). Shift with the arrows used to turn in larger steps (0.35 rad instead of 0.12), which is gone.
- The point the view turns around stays within the model's reach, half its diagonal from its centre (`maxTargetRadius`), so the model cannot be lost off screen. The controls glide with damping and kept the rest of a drag past that edge as a move still owed, which the on-demand render loop froze, so a drag back did nothing at first; the owed move (`_panOffset`, read defensively as the wheel listener already is) is now trimmed to the reach after every update.
- The zoom buttons and + and - scaled the camera's distance from the model's centre; they now zoom toward the panned point. Reset and Home first spend any turn or pan still gliding, which otherwise carried on from the starting view (0.30 units off in the test without it).
- Tests: `model-viewer.test.ts` covers the keys. `tests/model-viewer.spec.ts` drives a real scene with the right button, Shift and the left button, and two fingers sent as CDP touch events under the inline touch rule, then checks Reset during a glide, the reach and the drag back from it, the keyboard step, zoom around the panned point and the reach for keys; the reach and Reset checks fail without the trim and the spending. Another scenario presses Shift and Right on the inline viewer of a reply, which draws the model differently from Right alone, and Home, which draws the start again. The stage's radial gradient is dithered differently from frame to frame (815 pixels off by one), so that scenario draws the stage without it; it then passed 24 times in a row under four workers. A throwaway run with a tree model from the user's history (`artifacts/model-pan/`) showed the inline and expanded views panned, stopped at the reach, turned around the panned point, zoomed to the trunk and reset.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,259 files), 742 Vitest tests in 92 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check` (no Rust change, so Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun), and all 383 Edge and 10 WebKit browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained. Minecraft used three to four cores throughout: shard 2 failed `performance.spec.ts:653` on its first run, and `notifications.spec.ts:376` and `performance.spec.ts:540` on its second, none of which shows a model; each passed alone, and the third run of shard 2 passed whole.
- Not exercised: a real touch screen, including iOS Safari, where a sideways two-finger swipe should reach the inline viewer as Chromium's CDP touches do, and the native desktop app.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: Shift with the arrows for panning in place of the larger turning step, the reach as the limit, and panning keys that move the view rather than the model.

## A side panel stays with its chat — 2026-10-14

Requested 2026-10-14: "When opening a right sidebar, keep it open per chat (so switching between chats, don't close it, and the right sidebar open state is per chat)".

- The artifact side panel and the sub-agent panel belong to their chat (`artifactViews` and `subagentViews` in `+page.svelte`, `src/lib/side-panels.ts`): another chat, Connections or Settings hides the panel, and returning to its chat shows it again. Each chat keeps its own, closing one closes it for its chat alone, and the one-panel-at-a-time rule holds within each chat. An artifact in the modal still closes once its chat is left, as when a desktop notification opens another chat under it. Deleting a chat, here or through sync, and a workspace change drop its panels; a rewind still closes that chat's artifact.
- Shown again, the panel mounts afresh: an artifact's preview restarts, as after a reload, and a sub-agent's conversation opens at its latest update while it runs and at its task once finished. Panel widths are remembered as before.
- Focus: Edge and WebKit both move focus into a dialog on `show()`, not only `showModal()` (checked with a probe page), so a docked panel shown again with its chat puts focus back where it was (`showPanel`), while a drawer takes it as any modal does. A panel hidden with its chat used to send focus back to what it was opened from when it unmounted, taking focus off the sidebar row just chosen; now only its own Close or Escape returns focus.
- Tests: `tests/side-panels.spec.ts` covers two chats, each with its own docked panel, in Edge and WebKit (focus stays on the row chosen with the keyboard, closing one keeps the other, a Settings round trip returns beside the docked panel at the same paragraph and scroll position, the modal closes on a notification click), and a phone drawer that returns with its chat and takes focus. The ends of `subagent-panel.spec.ts` and `work-features.spec.ts` now return to the chat and find its panel, and `src/lib/side-panels.test.ts` covers leaving chats. With the focus handling taken out, the main scenario fails on the focused row in both engines.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,257 files), 736 Vitest tests in 91 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check` (no Rust change), and all 379 Edge and 8 WebKit browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained. The first run of shard 1 failed both variants of `chat-virtual-space.spec.ts:56` at its second hold (space 0, not above 150) while a Java process used about two cores; the test touches no panel, passed 30 times in a row on this change, and the rerun shard passed whole.
- Not exercised: the Viewer, which holds chats light and should show a sub-agent panel again only once it has read the chat whole, since a light reply holds no calls; and the real desktop app, as the change is page state alone.
