## Chats can be snoozed until the app starts again — 2026-10-14

Requested 2026-10-14: "Can you allow to snooze a chat, it archives a chat, but it shows on top as an option to either, close, open or snooze again when opening the app again (next app session)".

- Reading: every start of the desktop app already moves all chats to History, so a chat set aside is easy to lose among them. Taken as: Snooze moves a chat to History like Move to history and marks it, and once the app starts again (a new app session, as History counts them) the chat waits at the top of the sidebar until the user opens it, snoozes it again or closes the reminder.
- The workspace keeps the snoozes in the optional synced field `snoozes` (`src/lib/snoozes.ts`): per chat its id, its newest message's id when snoozed (`lastMessageId`) and when it was snoozed (`at`), stored by chat id and merged like folder icons (each side's changes since the base, the later snooze of one chat, an ended snooze yielding to one snoozed again, and a side without the list, such as the released relay, removing nothing and never written back after every foreign write). A snooze is due once `appSessions` holds a start later than it (`latestStart`), on any computer; reloads, a window shown from the tray and server hosts record no start. It ends by itself once the chat's newest message is no longer the one recorded, so sending a message, a retry or a rewind end it without comparing two devices' clocks; deleting the chat removes it, and the next write of the list drops snoozes ended that way while keeping those whose chat has not arrived from another device yet.
- Window (`+page.svelte`): **Snooze** in the chat toolbar after Move to history (`snooze-toggle`, pressed with the accent while the open chat is snoozed, a click cancels), and **Snooze**/**Cancel snooze** in a row's context menu between Fork conversation and Delete conversation (`ConversationContextMenu.svelte`). An Active chat goes through `archiveConversation`, so the open chat gives way to the next one in its folder from the toolbar and a row's action leaves the open chat, its draft, the view and the drawer in place; a History chat is snoozed where it is. Both are unavailable while the chat's reply runs. Due chats are listed between Conversations and the search (`.snoozed-chats`), the most recently snoozed first, as cards with the folder's icon, the title and **Open** (`openSnoozed`: ends the snooze and opens the chat in History, where a message brings it back to Active as before; the composer takes focus except on phones), **Snooze again** and **Close** (`leaveSnoozed`: focus moves to the card now in its place, else the search). History rows of snoozed chats end with a faint alarm and add Snoozed to their title and accessible name (`rowNotes`). Deleting a chat removes its snooze in the same save.
- Unit: `snoozes.test.ts` (due only after a later start, a reload's same start keeps it snoozed; a following message, a rewound last message, a missing chat or a fork's first message end it; the due list's order; snoozing again replaces; writes drop ended snoozes but keep those of chats not held yet; the schema refuses duplicates and malformed times or ids; the three-way merge, ties included; order-free comparison), `sync.test.ts` (merged through `mergeShared`, closed on one side, kept against an older relay, compared by `sameShared`/`sameMeta`, kept by a restored workspace) and `transport.test.ts` (an older relay that drops the list gets no upload for it alone, a change from another device leaves the snoozes here, and once the relay keeps the list the next change takes them there and an ended snooze goes by itself).
- Browser: `tests/snooze.spec.ts`, five scenarios against the desktop mock with app starts simulated by `test-app-session`: snoozed from the toolbar (the next chat opens, the History row is marked, the saved snooze names the last message), nothing on top after a reload, the card with its three choices after a start, Snooze again (back after the following start), Close (row unmarked, list empty); snoozed from an Active row's menu with another chat's draft kept, Open after a start (History selected, row current, composer focused), the toolbar snoozing and cancelling a History chat in place, and a message bringing it back to Active; a chat continued after its snooze, Cancel snooze from the menu keeping focus on the row, and a deleted snoozed chat leaving no snooze; three due chats handled from the keyboard with focus moving through the list to the search; and the phone drawer with 44 px choices and no horizontal overflow, also in WebKit. `move-to-history.spec.ts` lists Snooze second in the toolbar and `workspace.spec.ts` steps through Snooze in the menu. Dark, light, phone and WebKit captures of the cards, the History marks, the pressed toolbar alarm and the menu (`artifacts/snooze/`) were reviewed.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,253 files), 733 Vitest tests in 90 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`; Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun because no Rust file changed. All 377 Edge browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained and the 7 WebKit tests. The second shard first failed `notifications.spec.ts:376` (a phone push test that missed a 5 s wait), which passed 8 times out of 8 alone and the whole shard on its rerun.
- Not exercised: the native app, since the change is frontend only and the scenarios ran in Edge, the engine of WebView2. The released relay drops `snoozes` until the next release, so until then snoozes stay on the desktop that made them and the Viewer does not see them; the merge with an older relay is covered by the unit and transport tests, not by a real relay.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: Open opens the chat in History rather than returning it to Active, following the rule that only a sent message restores a History chat; Close ends the reminder and leaves the chat in History; the next start of the app on any computer wakes a snooze, and showing a hidden window from the tray does not; the cards sit above the search on every tab, the most recently snoozed first; a History chat can be snoozed too, as a reminder; no `/snooze` command and no snooze on a row's hover.

## A reply in a long chat no longer freezes the window every few seconds — 2026-10-13

Reported 2026-10-13: "I noticed really an apparent stuttering every 6 seconds, can you investigate what was causing this and address it?"

- Reading, on the installed 0.9.3 with its own data, read only: `workspace.json` held 155 chats in 88 MB; the largest, 9.8 MB with 34 messages and 10,038 recorded calls and comments, had a reply that ran six and a half hours that day. CPU time of the app's WebView2 renderer, sampled per thread every 50 ms while replies ran, showed the page's main thread busy 470 to 600 ms once a minute right after a workspace save, while the app's own process spent 1.4 s writing the 86 MB `sync-state.json`.
- Probe: the production build in headless Edge with the real chat copied, a relay on this computer and a mocked reply in that chat, timing every poll. While the reply only waited on one call, every poll blocked the page 416 to 578 ms and uploaded the whole conversation, 117.6 MB in 31 s. Through a 25 Mbit/s link each upload took 3.4 s, and the freeze came back every 5.0 s, every 7.5 s on a slower one: the reported stutter. A CPU profile split each poll: 37% comparing whole copies as JSON text (four texts of the chat in `mergeShared`), 27% writing it as text three more times (`syncPlan`), 20% validating it through the page's reactive proxies, 15% Tauri writing the upload as text. The running call's elapsed time, which the host sends every second, kept the chat marked, so a reply that only waited published it with every poll. While text streamed, the usage panel read every message's text again for each event, 8 ms each.
- Fix: syncs and saves read conversations through validated copies that share every message and block that did not change with the copy before (`src/lib/chat-snapshots.ts`); a change names the message it touched, as each run event does, and parts are replaced, never edited in place (`message.usage` in a compaction was the one exception). Copies are compared with `jsonEqual` (`src/lib/json-equal.ts`), which tells what comparing JSON texts tells and passes shared parts at once, and `retainQuestions` keeps a message that gains nothing as the same object. Uploads, workspace saves and checkpoint writes send their arguments as the bytes of their JSON, put together from parts already written (`src/lib/chat-bytes.ts`), which the app reads off its window thread (`src-tauri/src/sent.rs`; `relay::Sent` passes an upload's body on untouched). A checkpoint write carries only the conversations whose baseline changed, and the app completes the file from the one it replaces (`src-tauri/src/sync_state.rs`). Each conversation takes turns to upload: at most a third of the time and 512 KB a second on average, with a reply's end, a question and a chat the relay changed too going at once. A call's elapsed time marks its chat quiet (`applyRunEvent` returns `'clock'`, `marks.quiet`), published every 15 seconds or with any other change. The usage panel's next-request estimate is worked out only when read.
- After, same probe and build: text streaming at 20 events a second for 80 s through 25 Mbit/s gave no long task and no frame gap over 20 ms, uploads of the chat every 20 s (39 MB in 80 s), and the save and checkpoint as bytes (1 ms to encode, where writing them as text took 145 ms and 43 ms). With the whole 88 MB workspace loaded, 85 s of streaming gave no long task and no frame gap over 20 ms, and the checkpoint write carried that one chat. A reply waiting on a call that ticks every second: no long task, an upload every 20 s.
- Unit: `json-equal.test.ts` (8,000 random pairs, unrelated and nearly equal, against comparing `JSON.stringify` texts; shared parts never read), `chat-snapshots.test.ts` (copies equal a full parse; untouched messages and unchanged blocks shared; a thousand random events through `applyRunEvent` each checked against a full parse; bytes parse back to the same data and are written once per part), `change-marks.test.ts` (quiet marks; the conversation and message each change touched), `transport.test.ts` (a 4 MB chat waits its turn while a short one goes, a reply's end goes at once, a chat the relay changed merges at once, a ticking call goes after 15 s or with any other change, a checkpoint write carries the changed chat with the order of all and whole when the host asks). Rust: `sent.rs` (plain arguments, raw bytes and the arrays of numbers `postMessage` makes of them), `sync_state.rs` (completion from the file, unchanged conversations kept byte for byte, every refusal leaving the file as it was), `relay.rs` (an upload's body passed on byte for byte).
- Browser: every desktop test now reads a copy after each change and checks it against a full parse (`studioVerifySnapshots`, set by `mockAppCalls`), and `tests/global-teardown.ts` fails the run on a difference. Editing a call's record in place made `tool-progress.spec.ts` fail that way; no test of the suite found a difference. `performance.spec.ts`: the waiting reply now runs a call that ticks every second and still sends nothing for eight seconds (with ticks as ordinary changes it sent three uploads and failed); after a send every save and upload goes as bytes; a rename on another computer writes the checkpoint at once with that chat alone and the order of all 24.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,251 files), 725 Vitest tests in 89 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied, 482 Rust tests (32 opt-in ignored), all 372 Edge browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained (124 each) and the 6 WebKit tests. One WebKit test (`model-marks.spec.ts`, a Model picker click) timed out scrolling an option into view once, right after the Edge shards, and passed in two runs after it.
- Not exercised: the native app end to end. The bytes reach commands as Tauri's raw bodies, as `store_chat_image` has taken image bytes since 2026-10-11, and the commands' reading is unit-tested, but no QA build ran a reply in a long chat with a real CLI. The relay is unchanged, the Viewer keeps its own path, and a reply another computer runs is still downloaded whole with each update it publishes.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: the pacing (a third of the time, 512 KB a second, a second's slack), so other devices see a long chat's progress about every 20 s rather than with every poll; a running call's elapsed time reaching them every 15 s. Left for later: the relay still writes its whole workspace for each patch, and other devices still download a changed chat whole; uploading only what changed would need a new relay route.

## What a reply writes after waiting follows the text it waited with — 2026-10-13

Reported 2026-10-13, with a screenshot of a running reply: "I noticed that sometimes there are some messages that are being added to the history or before the latest message, I'm not sure if I misvisualized it or if it's an actual bug, can you investigate it?" In the screenshot the comments **Stopping the tiered pine's subagent now, then checking what it left.** and **Stopped. The subagent was far along…** and a command stand above the steer **Can you stop for now, we will continue later**, and under the steer stands **The cone tree (3 of 9) is done… The tiered pine (4 of 9) is building now.** with its model, which the reply had written before any of them.

- Reading: it is a bug, and what the screenshot shows is what the app drew. A Claude reply goes on through several turns when a turn ends while background work it waits for continues (`await_background_tasks`, a background agent, a workflow). The decoder reports each message's text as a `progress` comment and a turn's `result` as the reply's `text`, its answer, which the window keeps in one block and draws under the reply's work, leaving the comment with the same text out of the work. Nothing ended that answer when the next turn began: until that turn's own result arrived, everything it wrote and did was added to the work above the earlier turn's text, which read as the latest message. Once the turn ended, its text replaced the answer and the earlier text showed in the Work history where it was written, so a finished reply was in order and only a running one was not. A reply stopped or failed in the later turn kept the earlier text as its answer for good.
- Real data, read only: the reply in the screenshot is saved in the user's `workspace.json` (2026-10-03, complete, 1,561 recorded blocks). The cone tree text is its block 1,004, a turn's last message; the next 545 blocks are all calls of the background sub-agent that built the pine, while the reply waited; the steer started the turn recorded from block 1,550, whose comments and calls are the ones above the steer in the screenshot; and that turn's result, **Stopped. Here's where things stand…**, is the saved answer. 100 of the 471 replies in the 153 chats waited for background work at least once, 13 of them steered, and each showed this order whenever it went on after a wait. Five replies, all failed at a usage limit in a later turn on 2026-09-29, still hold an earlier turn's text as their answer.
- Fix on the computer that runs the reply: the decoder remembers that a turn ended with an answer (`answered` in `src-tauri/src/protocol.rs`), and with the first parent line of the next turn (a replayed user message, `system/init`, a stream event or an assistant message; never a sub-agent's line or a task notice) it sends an empty `text` event (`Decoder::turn_started`). The runner asks for it where it notices the turn, so the event goes out ahead of the steer's receipt, and the decoder's own check covers every other reader. The window then shows the earlier text as the comment the work holds it as, in its place, and what the new turn writes follows it. A steer taken while the reply waited records its place after that comment, so it stands below the text and above the turn it started. A reply stopped or failed in that turn ends with the last comment it wrote (`closingComment`), as any reply without an answer does, instead of keeping the earlier turn's text under work that came after it.
- Window: an empty `text` event empties the answer and never makes one (`applyRunEvent`), so an emptied answer is always a later state than the answer it held. `laterReply` (`src/lib/sync.ts`) takes it as the later of two copies level in status, blocks and usage revision: without that, a sync in the moment between the turn's first line and its first block took the copy that still held the answer, and brought the old text back under the new turn's work.
- Rust: `a_turn_that_follows_another_starts_the_replys_answer_again` decodes a reply through a wait, a sub-agent's lines and task notices (which empty nothing), a steer, a turn Claude starts by itself and a failed turn. Against the controlled CLI, `a_steer_while_the_reply_waits_follows_the_answer_it_was_sent_after` steers a reply that waits for a background agent and reads the order of its comments, answers and receipt, and `declared_background_tests_hold_the_reply_until_the_model_reports_them` now reads each mode's answers. With the fix switched off the three fail, the second with the order the screenshot shows: answer, steer, comment.
- Unit: `activity.test.ts` (an empty text makes no answer, empties one, and a relay job keeps the latest), `activity-groups.test.ts` (the reply's parts and answer through a wait, a steer, the turn after it, a stop there and its end), and `sync.test.ts` (the emptied copy is the later one on either side; it fails without the change in `laterReply`).
- Browser: `tests/reply-turns.spec.ts`, two scenarios against the desktop mock with the events the computer now sends. A reply that waited and went on shows its earlier text once, above what the new turn writes and runs, with no answer under the work until the turn's own; ended, the earlier text is in the Work history above the later comment. A reply steered while it waited shows the text in the part before the steer and the turn's comment and command after it, and stopped there ends with that comment while the text stays in the first part's history. Replaying the two with the events releases up to 0.9.3 sent fails both. Screenshots: `artifacts/reply-turns/before.png` and `after.png`, with the texts of the reported reply.
- Real CLI (Claude Code 2.1.296, Sonnet, through the production runner, opt-in): `installed_claude_steer_while_waiting_follows_the_answer_it_was_sent_after` reported the waiting turn's answer, the idle stretch ending, the emptied answer, the steer's receipt, then **STEERED** as a comment and as the answer. `installed_claude_reply_waits_for_declared_tests_but_not_servers` reported the waiting answer, the emptied answer as the CLI's own follow-up turn began, then the report of the tests, 26.6 seconds after the reply started. Neither left a process behind.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,244 files), 706 Vitest tests in 87 files, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied, 472 Rust tests (32 opt-in ignored), and all 378 browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained (126 each). The second shard first failed `notifications.spec.ts:376`, a relay test that does not touch this code, while a Blender job of another session held two cores; it passed six times alone and the whole shard passed when run again.
- Not exercised: the native app end to end, since a QA build has no Claude account signed in here; the runner with the real CLI and the window with the same events are tested apart. A reply followed from another device through the relay, where the job's latest `text` event and the merge are unit-tested. Codex and Gemini replies have one turn and are unchanged.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: the earlier turn's text returns to the reply's work as a comment, so after the reply ends it is in the Work history rather than kept in view as a second answer; visuals and files that text placed stay after the reply's prose, where they stand below the new turn's work while it runs, as files do in any reply until its answer places them, rather than moving into the comment; replies saved before this are not rewritten, so the five that failed in a later turn keep the earlier text as their answer.

## Accounts can be left out of balanced new chats — 2026-10-13

Requested 2026-10-13, after **Start on the least used account** shipped to `development` that day: "Can you allow to exclude one account from these load balance?"

- Reading: with the option on, every account of an agent that could answer on a computer took part, so an account kept for something else also received new chats. Taken as: each account can be left out of balancing, after which a new chat starts on it only when it is chosen for that chat.
- **Settings → New chats** lists, under the option and while it is on, the accounts balancing can choose between as checkboxes (`NewChatSettings.svelte`), checked unless left out. `balanceableAccounts` (`src/lib/account-balance.ts`) finds them: the accounts connected beside another account of their agent on some computer, so a single Codex account or the Gemini login is not listed. The page orders them agent by agent as the Agent picker does, gives each its color dot, and names an account's computers only when another account of that agent has the same name (`balanceChoices`).
- An unchecked account goes into the device-local `preferences.balanceExcluded` by account id, so it is left out on every computer where it is connected; the list is kept while the option is off, and an account that is gone leaves it with the next change. `balanceCandidates` drops those accounts, so balancing neither chooses nor reads them. A new chat whose account was last chosen by hand and is left out moves to another account as it opens, since its account is no candidate. With one candidate left new chats start there, and with none balancing does nothing, so they start with the account last chosen. The Agent picker still lists a left-out account, and choosing it there works as before.
- Unit: `account-balance.test.ts` adds the list (accounts beside another of their agent with the environments where they meet, one account connected twice, a lone account of an agent, an agent's accounts that never meet, a connection whose account is gone), and `domain.test.ts` keeps the left-out accounts with the option through a restore.
- Browser: `tests/account-balance.spec.ts` adds a scenario with three Claude accounts. Off, Settings shows no list; turned on, it lists the three, all checked, and neither the Codex account nor the Gemini login. Leaving out the least used account moves the open new chat to the next one and saves its account id; the next new chat avoids it too; chosen in the picker it stays through a return to the window, and the chat after that is balanced again; a restart keeps it out although it is then the account last chosen. Checked again, it is the least used account once more. With two accounts left out new chats start on the third, and with all three on the account last chosen, without the hover's **Account chosen by usage.** Two accounts named alike on two computers show **Desktop** and **Laptop** beside their names. Turned off, the list goes and the three left-out accounts stay saved. Reviewed `artifacts/balance-accounts.png`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,243 files), 704 Vitest tests in 87 files, the build, all 376 browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained (126, 125 and 125, no failure), `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 470 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored). The first Rust run reported one failure among the 470, whose name the filtered output did not keep; four runs after it passed all 470, and this change touches no Rust.
- Not exercised: the native app with real accounts, the Viewer, and a chat on another computer, as for the option itself.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: the list sits in Settings under the option rather than in each account's **Manage account**; an account is left out everywhere it is connected, not per computer; the list is kept on the device with the option, not synced; a draft already written under an account keeps it when that account is left out; accounts with nothing to be balanced against are not listed.

## New chats can start on the least used account — 2026-10-13

Requested 2026-10-13: "Can you have a option which is disabled by default, to allow the user to "load-balance" the new chats between accounts to try having a equal usage between the accounts, prioritize the 5-h limit or the weekly if don't have the 5-h limit".

- Reading: a new chat started on the account last chosen in the Agent picker, so spreading usage over several accounts of one agent meant choosing an account for every chat by its bars. Taken as: an option, off unless turned on, under which a new chat starts on the account of its agent that has used the least, compared by the 5-hour limit and, for accounts that report none (most Codex accounts), by the weekly one. The agent itself is not balanced: it stays the one last chosen.
- `src/lib/account-balance.ts` chooses (`balancedAccount`). Each account compares by the share used of the limits the chat's own bars show for its model (`visibleLimits`), a limit whose reset passed since its reading counted as unused. Accounts with room come first, then those without a reading, then those with a limit at 100% or refused by Claude over an account-wide limit until the reset it named. Accounts within 5 points of the least used are level (`levelPoints`) and take turns: the fewest replies running, then the lowest weekly usage, then the one that answered longest ago, then the picker's order. The replies running and the last answer of each account come from the saved chats (`accountActivity`).
- The page (`+page.svelte`) marks a new scratch chat `balanced` unless an account was named for it (**Chat** on an account's row in Connections; `/new` names the agent only). An effect chooses at once from the readings it has, asks for a reading of each account (`refreshBalance`, the Agent picker's readings, kept a minute natively), and chooses again once all arrived; with no reading at all, as at startup, the chat keeps its account until then. A choice made with every reading in hand is settled: `balancedAccount` keeps it while no other account is better by more than the tolerance, it moves only while the draft is empty, and returning to the window reads the accounts again. Choosing any account in the picker ends balancing for that chat (`changeSettings`' `chosen`). The option is the device-local `preferences.balanceAccounts`, set by **Settings → New chats** (`NewChatSettings.svelte`); the workspace's replicated part does not carry it.
- Real data, read only and printing shapes and counts alone: the user's `workspace.json` holds 17 accounts and 21 connections on three computers; 467 replies carry account readings, all from Claude: the 5-hour limit as a whole percentage with a reset time in 844 readings, and as 0 without one in 51, when no 5-hour window is open, which is why a reading without a reset compares as it reads. Since 2026-09-29 the four Claude accounts on Windows answered 143, 71, 69 and 24 replies. `accountActivity` took 0.04 ms a call over the 152 chats. From the last reading each account left with a reply, the choice was the account that had not answered since 2026-10-05.
- Unit: `account-balance.test.ts` (10 tests): the least used 5-hour limit, the weekly one without it, level accounts and their turns, the weekly limit between level accounts, limits used up and the order of accounts without a reading, a window that ended since its reading, a model's own limit only for that model, Claude's refusal with and without a reset, the settled choice, and the activity read from saved chats. `domain.test.ts` keeps the option off unless set and out of the replicated workspace.
- Browser: `tests/account-balance.spec.ts`, five scenarios against the desktop mock, each account reporting the usage the test gives it. Off, a new chat starts with the account last chosen; turned on, the open new chat moves at once, a new chat follows what the accounts used meanwhile, a restart keeps the option and balances the chat it opens, and turned off the last choice returns. An account chosen in the picker stays through a return to the window, a draft keeps its account while another account gains far more room and follows again once emptied, and **Chat** on an account's row names that account. Level accounts take turns over three sent chats, the weekly limit decides between them, and `/new` balances without remembering the account it was typed under. An account at 100% of its 5-hour or weekly limit and one that needs signing in are left out. Two Codex accounts without a 5-hour limit balance by the weekly one, and by the 5-hour one once they report it. Reviewed `artifacts/balance-settings-section-dark.png`, `-light.png` and `artifacts/balance-picker-dark.png`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,243 files), 703 Vitest tests in 87 files, the build, all 375 browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained (125 each, no failure, while two Blender jobs of other sessions ran), `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 470 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored).
- Not exercised: the native app with real accounts. The change is in the window alone and reads usage through the reader the Agent picker already uses, and an isolated QA build has none of the user's accounts signed in. The Viewer and a new chat on another computer, whose readings travel through the relay, run the same code and were not driven here.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: the option belongs to the device rather than the workspace, like the account last chosen; only accounts are balanced, never agents; the 5-point tolerance and the turns level accounts take, so chats started together do not all start on one account; an account never changes under a draft; any choice in the picker ends balancing for that chat, and the picker has no entry for "least used"; a draft restored from an earlier session keeps its account; existing chats are never moved; the label **Start on the least used account** under **New chats**, and **Account chosen by usage.** in the Agent control's hover.

## A steer is a message of your own, where the reply took it — 2026-10-13

Requested 2026-10-13: "Can you make the steer message show as it was a separated message sent from me (but mark it as a steer instead of a common message)".

- Reading: an accepted steer was drawn inside the reply, as a quoted block headed **You · Steered this reply** between the reply's Work history and its answer, wherever in the reply it had arrived, and while the reply ran it stayed under all the live activity, below calls made after it. Taken as: a steer reads as one of your own messages, your bubble at the conversation's right edge, told from an ordinary message by a **Steer** mark, and it stands in the conversation where it was sent, with the reply's earlier work above it and the reply going on below.
- The reply stays one saved message, drawn in parts (`replyParts` in `src/lib/activity-groups.ts`, `MessageView.svelte`): the work recorded before the first steer, then each steer followed by what the reply recorded until the next. A part is the reply's avatar and heading with a `ToolActivity` of its own, live rows while the reply runs and a closed **Work history** afterwards; the answer, questions, footer and actions follow the last part. A part without visible work shows only its steer, so a steer taken before the reply's first call stands above the reply and two taken in a row stand together. `ToolActivity` takes a part's entries (`work`) and `continued`, which folds the part's latest call into its group like any other and leaves **Stopped**, **Failed** and **Limit reached** to the last part. Until something follows a steer, the last part shows the waiting dots under **Responding**.
- Place: a reply kept its steers as receipts with a sequence and no place. `applyRunEvent` records one as a steer first arrives: `after`, the number of calls, comments, notes and reasoning blocks the reply holds then, and `at`, the time its computer confirmed the input. Places live in a list of their own, `steeringPlaces`, beside the receipts, because the receipt schema is strict: a receipt holding one more field fails the app and the relay of every earlier release, which refuse the whole chat, while an unknown message field is only dropped. For the same reason the host sends the time beside the receipt in the `steering` event, and `retainRunEvent` keeps it in the job other devices follow. Copies of a reply merge their places (`mergeSteeringPlaces`: the larger count, the earlier time, one per steer in id order, so every copy holds the same list) on both merge paths of `sync.ts`. Steers keep the order they were sent in whatever each copy counted, and one without a place follows the reply's work.
- A reply that ended without an answer shows its last comment in its place. After a steer that comment would read as the reply's answer to it, so `closingComment` returns it only when no steer followed it; otherwise it stays in the Work history of its part.
- Imports: the Codex converter appended a turn's mid-turn messages after all of the turn's records. It now leaves each among them where the turn's items had it, and the import names the reply's run on them (`steered_by`), so an imported reply divides where the message was sent.
- Real CLIs (`scripts/steering-cli-probe.mjs`, Codex CLI 0.162.1 and Claude Code 2.1.296, an eight-second shell wait, both replies ending with the corrected text): Codex answered `turn/steer` while the command still ran, then reported the steer's own user message, reasoning, the command's end and the answer; Claude Code replayed the steer right after the command's result and before its next message. So a steer stands below the command it interrupted in a Codex reply, which finishes above it, and after the call that was running in a Claude reply, in both before anything written in answer to it.
- Real data, read only and printing counts alone: 21 of the 463 replies in the 151 chats of the user's `workspace.json` hold a steer, one each, all Claude, 20 complete and one stopped, 21 to 81 characters on one line. None has a place, since no release recorded one, so each shows its steer after its Work history and before its answer, where it was drawn before, now as a bubble under a heading without a time.
- Unit: `steering.test.ts` adds the place and time recorded on first arrival (the answer not counted, a late copy changing nothing, a steer already held left without one, another run's steer leaving nothing, the job keeping the time), the strict receipt against a place inside it with the list surviving a restore and the shared schema, and merges (larger count in either order, earlier time, a copy without places, the same list whichever copy merges, and through `mergeShared` on each path). `activity-groups.test.ts` divides a reply at one and two steers, before anything shown, twice in a row, without a place, past what a copy holds, with places out of order or missing among placed ones, with a compaction at the same place and without recorded work, and covers `closingComment`. `transport.test.ts` syncs with a relay that drops the places from every save, and `imports.test.ts` places an imported steer after the command it followed. Rust: the `steering` event serializes the time beside a receipt of exactly four fields, the Codex converter orders comment, command, steer and answer, and `steered_by` names the reply on coalesced events.
- Browser: `tests/steering.spec.ts` keeps its three scenarios and adds five. At desktop and phone width, in Edge and WebKit: while the reply runs, the read and comment made before the steer stay in the first part, the steer follows as a group named Steering message with the **Steer** mark above a bubble whose right edge and background match the user's own message, and the command made afterwards appears in a second part headed with the model and a time, the only one reading **Responding**; once the reply ends each part has its own Work history holding only its work, the answer is in the last, nothing overflows, the saved place is 3 with a time beside a receipt of four fields, and a reload shows the same. A reply saved without a place shows all its work, then the steer, then the answer under a heading without a time. A reply stopped after a steer shows the waiting dots, then **Response stopped.** in its last part, with the earlier comment only in the first part's history. Two steers before any visible work stand together above the reply's only heading. Screenshots at one and a half times the scale, both themes: `artifacts/steering/`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,239 files), 692 Vitest tests in 86 files, the build, all 370 browser tests in three shards started with the local sockets drained (124, 123 and 123), `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 470 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored). The second shard first failed three timed scenarios, while another job on this computer (Blender and four Python workers, each holding a core) ran beside it: `performance.spec.ts:426` measured 157 to 197 ms a task against its 150 ms budget, `performance.spec.ts:483` 30 to 39 ms an update against 25, and `notifications.spec.ts:376` missed a five-second wait once in four runs alone. With this change set aside the two performance tests measured the same under that load, 150 to 225 ms and 31 to 38 ms, four runs each. Once the job ended the whole shard passed, and with the change the three passed four of four alone, at 68 to 99 ms and 15 ms. Logs: `artifacts/steering/logs/`.
- Not exercised: the native app, since `scripts/steering-native-smoke.mjs` needs terminal logins this computer lacks for Claude (its selector and a check of the saved place were updated, unrun); a reply followed through the relay from another computer, where only the merge and the job's event are tested; the Viewer on a phone. The production relay drops `steeringPlaces` until the next release reaches it, so until then a place survives on the computer that ran the reply and a steer can fall back to the end of the work elsewhere.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: the reply goes on under a repeated heading rather than flowing on without one; the mark is the word **Steer** with a ship's wheel above the bubble, and the bubble takes the accent border; no Rewind here on a steer; a steer taken before any visible work stands above the reply; replies steered on earlier releases keep their steer after their work rather than at a guessed place; the composer still holds the text as **Sending…** until the provider acknowledges it, so nothing unconfirmed is drawn as a message.

## A compaction is part of the reply's Work history — 2026-10-12

Requested 2026-10-12, with a screenshot of **Context compacted automatically · 967.265 tokens before → 8.081 after** on a line of its own between an open Work history and the answer: "Can you make the compact be part of the history instead of it showing on the top of the last conversation?"

- Reading: the marker of a compaction that happened while a reply worked was drawn after the reply's Work history and above its answer, wherever among the calls it had happened, and stayed there with the history closed. Taken as: the marker is an entry of the reply's work, at the point where the compaction began, and nothing but the history stands above the answer. A `/compact` reply is left as it was, since its marker is what that reply did.
- A reply kept its markers in a list of their own (`compactions`), with nothing saying where each came among its calls. `applyRunEvent` now records that as a marker first arrives: `after`, the number of calls, comments, notes and reasoning blocks the reply holds then, everything but its answer. `activityEntries` places each marker by that count among the reply's blocks, the hidden ones included, and `groupActivityEntries` keeps it as a boundary between groups of calls, like a comment. `ToolActivity` draws it as a quiet row aligned with the group labels: a spinner and **Compacting context…** while it runs, then a fold icon with **Context compacted automatically** and the sizes the provider reported, or **Compaction was not confirmed** once the reply ended without one. `MessageView` keeps its own line only for `/compact` replies.
- Sync: copies of a reply keep a marker's place when they merge (`mergeCompactions`). A device that follows a reply through the relay hears its events from the job, which keeps a window of them (64 comments, 64 reasoning blocks, the newest 160 calls), so it can count fewer entries before a marker than the computer that ran the reply. The larger count wins on either side, so two devices settle on one place instead of each sending its own back after the other's write, and a copy without a place, as a relay on an older release returns it, removes none.
- Real data, read only and printing counts alone: of the 452 replies in the 150 chats of the user's `workspace.json`, 30 in 23 chats hold 31 markers, all automatic and confirmed, none from `/compact`, and none with a place, since no release recorded one. The reply in the screenshot (476 calls, 64 comments, 16 reasoning blocks) opened in the test mock at twice the scale: closed, its history is followed directly by the answer; open, the marker is its last row, above Collapse. The same reply with a place put at its 300th entry shows the marker between a failed group of calls and the comment after it. Opening the history took 163 and 170 ms.
- Unit: `compaction.test.ts` adds the labels and sizes, the place recorded on first arrival (the answer not counted, a later copy of the start changing nothing, a place that came with the event kept, a marker already held without one left without) and merges (the larger place in either order, a copy without one whichever is newer, and through `mergeShared` on each path). `activity-groups.test.ts` places markers between two calls, before the first entry, after reasoning, two at one place, unplaced and past what a copy holds, and alone in a reply without other work. `transport.test.ts` syncs with a relay that drops the place from every save. With the merge keeping only the newer copy's place, three of those fail.
- Browser: `tests/compaction.spec.ts` adds an automatic compaction between two groups of calls, at desktop and phone width. While the reply runs, the marker follows the calls made so far, which fold into their group, reads **Compacting context…** with a spinner and then the sizes, and the calls after it start a group below it. Once the reply ends nothing stands between the closed history and the answer and the marker is out of the page; the open history lists the note, the comment, **Read 2 files**, the marker and **Edited 1 file and ran 1 command** in that order, without overflowing the reply; the saved place is 4; a reload shows the same; and the same reply saved without a place shows the marker last, after one merged group. The existing `/compact` scenarios still pass unchanged. Screenshots: `artifacts/compaction/automatic-*.png`, and of the marker's three states in both themes, `artifacts/compaction-history/`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,239 files), 686 Vitest tests in 86 files, the build, all 363 browser tests, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 469 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored). Three earlier full browser runs each failed one scenario, a different one each time. `performance.spec.ts:483` measured 25.4 ms a sub-agent panel update against its 25 ms budget while four workers ran; alone it averaged 13 to 14 ms in six runs with this change and 13 to 16 ms without it. `composer-growth.spec.ts:98` and `questions.spec.ts:11` timed out on a page that never loaded; the second's trace shows `.svelte-kit/generated/root.js` failing with `net::ERR_NO_BUFFER_SPACE`. A third of the suite alone leaves about 1,500 local sockets in TIME_WAIT, so a run started with none can still lose a page: the last two runs waited for them to drain before each third (`--shard=N/3`), and both passed, the second on the final code. The four compaction scenarios also passed 32 of 32 in eight repeats. Logs: `artifacts/compaction-history/logs/`.
- Not exercised: a real automatic compaction, which needs a context near its window (the decoders have fixture coverage); the native app; a reply followed through the relay from another computer, where only the merge is tested. The production relay drops the place until the next release reaches it, so until then a marker's place survives on the computer that ran the reply and can fall back to the end of the history elsewhere.
- Choices of mine, not confirmed: `/compact` replies keep their marker as their content; replies saved earlier show theirs at the end of their history rather than at a guessed place; the fold icon; and a reply whose only recorded work is a compaction gets a Work history for it.

## Each account has a color of its own — 2026-10-12

Requested 2026-10-12: "Can you make each account have a different color, so it's more visible which account I'm in?"

- Reading: nothing but its name told one account from another, and the user's are email names that begin alike, which the Agent control cut at 220px. Taken as a color for each account, shown wherever the app names it and most of all on the Agent control and in its list. Their `workspace.json` holds 17 accounts (10 Claude, 5 Codex, 2 Gemini) on three computers, three of them labels without a connection.
- `src/lib/account-colors.ts`: ten named colors, each with a dark and a light value (`--account-<name>` in theme.css), and one per account in the synced workspace field `accountColors`. An account without one takes the color the fewest accounts beside it have, counting first the accounts of its agent on its computers, then any account there, then its agent's elsewhere, then all; `AccountMark.svelte` draws the agent's glyph on it in the Agent picker's choices, on the Agent trigger in place of the bot icon and on a new chat's welcome, and `.account-dot` precedes the account's name in Connections, the Next message hint and the Switched to notice. Manage account offers the palette as a radio group. An agent with one account on a computer keeps its own mark.
- Sync: merged like folder icons, except that a color the user chose beats one an account took by itself, then the later choice, then the relay's, whatever the baseline holds, and a color taken here that the relay never saw moves when the relay's colors leave the account a better one. An older relay that drops the list loses nothing and starts no write loop.
- Real data, read only and printing counts and color names alone: the accounts on Windows get 9 different colors for 9, those in the WSL distribution 9 for 9 and the server's 3 for 3. Counting accounts without their computers, as a first version did, left two pairs on Windows with the same color.
- Found on the way. A cap on the toolbar's triggers left the room a longer name asked for empty beside them (19px at 1380px wide, 69px at 1000px): the picker now carries the cap, and the Agent's is 280px, so the user's longest name, 20 characters, shows whole. Playwright's WebKit draws the Claude glyph as the green emoji, which would cover the color; the catalog's glyph now carries the text selector. That WebKit build ignores the selector, so its effect on Apple's engines is not confirmed.
- Two existing tests failed in full runs, both also on the untouched code. `rewind.spec.ts:142` read the first request again when the second reply had not started (5 of 40); it now waits for the reply (60 of 60). `chat-days.spec.ts:155` on a phone in WebKit ended 72px short of the chat's end (5 of 60), a fault of the app: a scroll event arrives a frame after the scroll it reports, WebKit delivered the event of the chat's own return to its end after the window had become narrower, and reading the position then stopped following. `scrolled` in `+page.svelte` now leaves following alone for an event that reports the position the chat was last read at or put at (60 of 60, and the probe that caught it 60 of 60).
- Unit: `account-colors.test.ts` (11 cases: a value per theme for every color, a color each while the palette lasts, the same colors whatever order a device holds accounts in, kept colors, the accounts one computer lists together, choices, malformed lists, merges with and without the list, two devices that took the same colors, and a color moved only for a better one), `sync.test.ts` and `transport.test.ts` (an older relay that drops the list, then one that keeps it).
- Browser: `tests/account-colors.spec.ts` adds a second Claude account and reads each account's color on the Agent list, the trigger, the welcome and Connections, with Codex keeping its own mark; changes a color with the mouse and the keyboard, cancels, fails a save and reloads; switches account inside a chat and reads the hint and the notice; and checks, in Edge and WebKit, that a long name leaves no empty room after the Agent control. `tests/federation.spec.ts` pairs two computers through a real relay: their six accounts end with six colors, and a color chosen on one reaches the other. Screenshots: `artifacts/account-colors-{picker,new-chat,connections,palette,switch,toolbar}.png`; dark, light, phone and WebKit views came from throwaway scripts in `artifacts/account-colors/`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors and 0 warnings in 5,239 files), 681 Vitest tests in 86 files, the build, all 361 browser tests, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 469 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored). Five earlier full runs each failed one or two scenarios: the two tests above, and three page loads that ended in `net::ERR_NO_BUFFER_SPACE` or `net::ERR_ADDRESS_IN_USE`, since full runs back to back left up to 1,900 local sockets in TIME_WAIT. The final run started once they had drained. Logs: `artifacts/account-colors/logs/`.

Requested 2026-10-12: "Show a division when a chat is from another day".

- Reading: taken as the open chat's messages rather than the sidebar. A reply shows only its time of day and a question none, so nothing told yesterday's messages from this morning's, and two of the user's four Active chats had started the day before and continued that morning. The sidebar already has the day in History's session headings, and Active lists each folder's chats with nothing to divide by day.
- `chatDays` in `src/lib/chat-days.ts` gives each message that begins another day than the message before it its day, comparing the first message with today, and the page renders a `.message-day` separator above that message: the day's name from `dayLabel`, as History names its days, between two hairlines, with the full date on hover. It sits between messages, so the reading position kept across Connections and Settings, which reads the column's messages, skips it. Nothing is saved or synced.

None falls above a reply instead of a question, and the two Active chats continued from the day before read Yesterday at their top and Today above that morning's first message. Only counts were printed.

- Browser: `tests/chat-days.spec.ts` sends three questions, saves them as asked three days ago, the day before and today, and reads the conversation in order with its three divisions, the separator role and name, the full date on hover and no new division for another message today. A chat from the day before opens with Yesterday, gains Today above a new question and keeps following its end; each division spans the conversation with its name in the middle, clear of the messages around it, at desktop and phone width in Edge and WebKit. A chat open half a minute before midnight gains Yesterday one second into the next day, which fails without the new timeout. Screenshots: `artifacts/chat-days-edge.png`, `-webkit`, `-phone-edge` and `-phone-webkit`; dark and light close-ups came from a throwaway run.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors in 5,235 files), 668 Vitest tests, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 469 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored). All 355 browser tests passed on the first run. A rerun after a comment changed failed `file-changes.spec.ts:201`, which found 3px between the Files edited and usage toggles on a phone, on a page without a division: the test read the two buttons in separate calls while the chat still glided to the end of the reply just sent (a probe saw it scroll for 136 ms after the reply ended). That scenario and the Memories one now read both buttons in one frame, passed 40 and 24 times in a row, and the final run of all 355 passed. Logs: `artifacts/chat-days/`.

## Running replies survive a WebView2 network service restart, and images attach after it — 2026-10-11

Requested 2026-10-11: "I got this two errors on all my chats, investigate what happened and fix it", with a reply that failed after 57 minutes with **This conversation is already responding. Stop it or wait for it to finish.**, and a draft whose image showed **The images could not be kept: Send the image bytes**.

- Cause: two replies, 44 and 57 minutes in, failed at the same moment (17:41:31) while their CLIs ran on, and Windows logged a new WebView2 process 0.25 s before. Tauri's IPC script posts each call with `fetch`; when one fails, it sends the call again through `postMessage` and keeps `postMessage` until the page reloads. The copies of both replies' `run_agent` calls were refused as second replies, and the refusal answered the page. `postMessage` carries only JSON, so every image body afterwards arrived as an array of numbers and was refused. A scratch Tauri 2.11.5 app showed that ending the WebView2 network service sends every pending call again 1 ms after the new one starts, while ending the GPU process or the storage service changes nothing. Restarting the app at 17:46, which also installed 0.9.2, cleared both. See docs/DIAGNOSTICS.md.
- Fix: the page names each call in a `studio-call` header (`src/lib/window-answers.ts`), which the copy keeps. Commands that act run their work once per name (`src-tauri/src/calls.rs`), and a copy gets the first arrival's answer. These are replies, file Undo, question answers, MCP forms, steering, sign-ins and their codes, titles, folder icons, console runs, screen actions, CLI installs and update checks, plugin, MCP and account management, Claude file sharing, exports and artifact downloads. `store_chat_image` takes the bytes raw or as numbers, and raw answers are read either way (`answerBytes`). The page records the move as an `ipcFallback` report, and the app records each WebView2 process that fails as a `webviewProcess` report, in `diagnostics/`.
- Stack: the first native run crashed the QA build with a stack overflow as the reply started. Wrapping `run_agent`'s work had made its future 182 KB in a debug build, and Tauri copies it several times on the window thread's stack. `once` now keeps the work on the heap, which makes it 1.5 KB (60 KB before this change), and a `lib.rs` test keeps it under 16 KB.
- Native: `scripts/ipc-replay-native-smoke.mjs` ran real Codex replies in an isolated build. Failing the reply's fetch 10 s in passed once. Ending the QA app's network service passed three times out of four; in the fourth, Codex exited at the start of its reply, before writing any log line. Each passing reply completed with its answer, and the reports were written, the network service as `utilityProcessExited`, `crashed`, "Network Service". An image then stored and read back through `postMessage`. The build before this change failed the same reply with **This conversation is already responding**, and storing an image after the move with **Send the image bytes**.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors in 5,232 files), 654 Vitest tests, the build, 348 browser tests, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 469 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored). Logs: `artifacts/ipc-replay-verify/`.
- Not verified: why the network service ended on 2026-10-11, which nothing recorded; the next time, a `webviewProcess` report will. After the move to `postMessage`, a model file read in its own window arrives as JSON numbers, which a model of hundreds of megabytes may be too large for.

## The Model picker's marks keep their size on macOS and Linux — 2026-10-11

Requested 2026-10-11: "My friend that has the app downloaded complained about this layout bug, can you fix it?", with a screenshot of a Codex chat's Model picker showing each choice's name as a column of single letters beside a wide band, an empty Model trigger, and the selected model's print drawn over the Agent picker.

- Cause: the macOS and Linux apps draw with WebKit (WKWebView, WebKitGTK), as does the Viewer on an iPhone. WebKit measured a mark before it resolved its print square's `height: 100%`, so `aspect-ratio: 1` gave the square no width, and the print's SVG, which has no size of its own, counted as an SVG's default 300px. The Model trigger's chip came out 300px wide, and the trigger, which centers its content, pushed it 53px over the Agent picker while the name beside it shrank to nothing. Each picker pill came out 319 to 329px wide, leaving its name a 9px column in a 182px row. Playwright's WebKit reproduced the screenshot. Edge, whose engine WebView2 shares, drew 20px and 26px squares, so Windows never showed it.
- Fix: `ModelMark.svelte` gives the chip's and the pill's print square its size outright (`--mark-size`, 20px and 26px). The reply avatar's tile keeps `aspect-ratio`, which WebKit already drew correctly. In WebKit the trigger, pills and names now measure exactly as in Edge, at desktop and phone widths.
- Browser: `playwright.config.ts` now has an `edge` project, which runs every test as before, and a `webkit` project for tests tagged `@webkit`. The picker scenario of `tests/model-marks.spec.ts` is tagged and also checks that the trigger's print is a square inside the trigger, with the whole name after it. Against the previous component it failed in WebKit (the chip 300px wide where 20px was expected) and passed in Edge. Screenshots of the Codex and Claude Model pickers, the Agent picker with its usage bars, the usage panel, Connections and Settings were reviewed in WebKit, with no other difference from Edge. `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` notes the WebKit project.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors in 5,232 files), 651 Vitest tests, the build, 348 browser tests (347 in Edge, 1 in WebKit) on the first run and `cargo fmt --check` passed. Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun because no Rust file changed. Log: `artifacts/model-marks-webkit/verify.log`.
- Not exercised: the macOS and Linux apps themselves. Playwright's WebKit on Windows stood in for WKWebView and WebKitGTK.

Requested 2026-10-11: "Can you allow to archive a scratch (show the button to archive, but in practice it just deletes the chat)?"

- Each scratch chat's sidebar row is now a `.conversation-row` like a conversation's and ends with the same `.conversation-archive` button: the archive icon on hover or keyboard focus, always on touch screens, 44 px on phones, with the title making room. A scratch chat has no history to move to, so the button is named "Discard draft: <title>" and `archiveRow` in `+page.svelte` passes it to `discardScratch` at once, without the context menu's confirmation. Another scratch chat is removed; the open one empties where it is and keeps its computer, folder and agent. Focus moves on to the row now in its place, and the open chat, its draft, the view and the phone drawer stay. The context menu's confirmed **Discard draft** is unchanged, and the toolbar still offers Move to history only for conversations.
- Browser: `tests/drafts.spec.ts` adds a scenario with three scratch chats in a project. The button is hidden at rest and shows on hover at the row's end, sized and placed as on a conversation's row. It discards another scratch chat without a dialog while the open one keeps its text and the saved drafts follow, and leaves History empty. From the keyboard it empties the open one, keeping its folder, with focus on the next row. On a phone it shows on the scratch row at 44 px and keeps the drawer open. Screenshots: `artifacts/scratch-discard-hover.png` and `artifacts/scratch-discard-phone.png`.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors in 5,232 files), 651 Vitest tests, the build, 347 browser tests on the first run and `cargo fmt --check` passed. Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun because no Rust file changed. Logs: `artifacts/scratch-discard/`.
- Not exercised: the native app. The change is frontend-only, and the scenarios ran in Edge, the engine of WebView2.

## The VPS reads as online, and Claude logins survive the night — 2026-10-11

Requested 2026-10-11: "I tried start a chat on my VPS, but it says it's offline, even though it should not, also the renew token still not working, that the third time I had to login on my account after the update".

- VPS offline: `agent-studio-host.service` had run for 13 hours and answered every relay request, but the relay's saved state showed its `metaRevision` moving with every revision and only the order of the fleet's lists changing: the desktop kept `Desktop, VPS` and the VPS host, which added itself before it took in the workspace, `VPS, Desktop`, and `syncPlan` compared the settings in order, so each poll on each side uploaded its order, then applied and checkpointed the other's, saving the 69 MB workspace each time. Two minutes of loopback capture on the VPS found a 16.9-second gap between the host's heartbeats, past the relay's 15-second presence, and its watchdog had recorded a 14-second window stall a minute before the report; the heartbeat went out only after a poll's sync, and not at all when it failed. `incremental-sync.test.ts` reproduces the two orders (it fails on the previous code), and `transport.test.ts` checks that a failed sync still sends the heartbeat and that a poll finding the last one still syncing sends one.
- Sign-in every day: the lost logins on this computer held `accessToken: ""`, `refreshToken: ""`, `expiresAt: 0` beside an untouched `refreshTokenExpiresAt`, which Claude Code 2.1.296 writes only when a renewal gets `invalid_grant` (`tengu_oauth_refresh_token_marked_dead_invalid_grant`). A fake OAuth server behind a local proxy with a throwaway certificate authority (`platform.claude.com/v1/oauth/token` spending a refresh token on arrival and refusing a spent one), with the installed CLI in a throwaway profile, showed `claude auth status` (and `claude plugin list --json`) sending a renewal at 150 ms and exiting 45 ms later, before the new token arrived, every time; the next renewal then got `invalid_grant` and the login was cleared, as `auth status` then reported. The model catalog query killed with `taskkill /F` lost a renewal slower than its 1.7-second wait the same way, and left Claude Code's lock (`.oauth_refresh.lock`, stale after 60 seconds) behind. With every connection refused, `auth status` answered in 0.2 s and left the token valid; with the new end, a 5-second renewal held the catalog query's input open until its lock went, saved the new token, and the next process stayed signed in. Rust tests cover the end (a CLI finishing what it saves, a live lock holding the input, a stale one not) and the refused connections.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors in 5,232 files), 651 Vitest tests, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy with warnings denied and 461 Rust tests (31 opt-in ignored). The browser suite passed 344 of 346 on the first run: `private-workspaces.spec.ts:370` caught a stale Viewer tab sending a heartbeat after its sync failed with `workspace_changed`, so only desktops heartbeat after a failed sync now, and `viewer-light-chats.spec.ts:132` missed its first sign-in page under four workers; both passed alone after the change.
- Not verified: a real renewal against Anthropic's server (the fake server follows the requests the installed CLI sent), and the VPS host on the fixed release, which waits for it.

## The VPS runs chats as a server host — 2026-10-10

Requested 2026-10-10: "allow the VPS to also be considered as a host? So I can start chats and do things on the VPS/Server where the relay lives". The user chose, when asked, a user of its own with sudo (then the `claude` user another session was setting up on the VPS for Unreal and Blender work), sign-in from the app for both Codex and Claude, and setup that happens by itself with the relay's.

- Platform: the released v0.8.1 AppImage, extracted, ran under `dbus-run-session` and `xvfb-run` as a throwaway user on the VPS (Debian 13): its page ran and saved its workspace, its window opened on display :100, it took about 750 MB (app 190 MB, WebKit 420 MB, network process 62 MB, Xvfb 77 MB) and 0.3% CPU idle. No packages were missing.
- Server mode (`src-tauri/src/server.rs`): Rust tests cover the switch, the key file read at each connection and the server's watchdog count. `transport.test.ts` checks that a restarted server interrupts only its own reply and leaves a desktop's running, and that a server never reports a chat as viewed while a desktop does.
- Remote sign-in: the Codex app-server's own types and a throwaway `chatgptDeviceCode` start (cancelled) confirmed the device-code answer (`https://auth.openai.com/codex/device`, a `XXXX-XXXXX` code) and its `account/login/completed` end. Rust tests drive stand-in CLIs started from elsewhere: Codex signs in by device code with nothing opened, Claude runs with `BROWSER=true` and takes the pasted code. `remote-sign-in.test.ts` follows a sign-in from the device that opens it (page, later states, code and Cancel through the relay) and on the computer that runs it (no page opened, one latest event, cancel, codes refused for another sign-in). `relay.test.ts` and `web.test.ts` check the jobs' arguments, that they leave once read, the heartbeat's features, and that a browser cannot claim them. `tests/federation.spec.ts` adds a Codex account on a server from a desktop through a real relay: the desktop shows the code and the page, and the account reads connected once the server's CLI reports success.
- Updater: `vps-update.test.ts` covers deployment after the relay, the wait for replies, refused AppImages, releases that cannot run as a server, a release tracked before the AppImage was recorded, provisioning retries, the rollback, key rotation, the unit and the settings. `scripts/vps/smoke.mjs --appimage` ran on the VPS with this branch's AppImage, built in a throwaway Debian 13 container there. The updater authenticated it, extracted it and started it as `agent-studio-qa-host`; the app paired with the isolated relay as a server (`features: ['signIn', 'server']`) and listed its home folder for another device. The next release waited for a running reply and then moved both the relay and the host, and a host that exited went back to the one before. The first run found that the AppImage unpacks two folders as `0700`, which the build user could not enter once they were root's (841369e). Production services and releases were unchanged.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors), 648 Vitest tests, the build, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy, 456 Rust tests, the VPS smoke (11 checks, cleaned up) and 346 browser tests, of which `notifications.spec.ts:376` failed under the full suite's four workers as it does on the unmodified HEAD (one run in four) and passed 8 runs of 8 alone on both. Logs: `artifacts/vps-update-qa/`.
- Not verified: a real provider sign-in and reply on the VPS, which wait for the release that deploys the host there.

## Artificial limits are gone — 2026-10-10

Requested 2026-10-10. A 512 MiB memory cap took the relay down for 21 hours. The user asked to "just remove these caps… the moment something freezes or crashes I know it reached it's limit not an artificial limit", then, shown the app's own limits, "Yes, remove them all".

- Server: the relay's unit has no `MemoryMax`, `CPUQuota` or `TasksMax` and sets `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=32768`. Release builds run without memory, task or time limits (176f31c, after the VPS smoke test, with the updater reinstalled from it).
- Inventory: three read-only agents listed about 400 limits across the Rust host, the frontend, the relay and the docs. Every cap on the size, number or duration of content and work went:
  - Run requests: 200 messages and 400 KB of history, instructions, mentions, skills, fallback models, thinking and compaction ranges.
  - CLI output: the 2 MB line limit that failed a reply.
  - Attachments and shown files: images (16 × 16 MiB), visuals and previews, and send_files (8 files, 12 groups, 16 MiB, 1 GiB, 30 s). Another device now reads a model whole and falls back to its views only when that read fails, instead of always showing views above 12 MiB.
  - Records: tool results (512 KB / 8 MB), activity records, and file changes, including the 100 Claude edits per reply after which edits went missing and Undo reported success. Also Undo itself, transcripts (4 GiB), screens and console code.
  - Interaction: questions, forms, steering, plans and workflows; the queue (8), drafts, templates, app sessions and folder icons.
  - Discovery and inspection lists, plugins, MCP, shared context and live usage.
  - Relay: uploads, job bodies, updates, events, counts, file guards and job ages (remote Claude/Codex runs were cut off at 61 minutes).
  - Host timeouts on work that may still be progressing: Gemini's 5-minute reply deadline, titles, sign-ins, MCP, plugins, models, updates and WSL lookups.
- Kept, as CLAUDE.md's new first rule says:
  - The relay's limits before sign-in: the 4 KB pairing body, 10 attempts a minute, and now a 30-second idle cutoff while that body is read, since Node's request timeout is off.
  - Liveness: Stop's grace, the relay's 45-second job liveness, and connect and idle network checks. The desktop's relay client adds TCP keepalive, so a dead connection fails while a slow one does not.
  - Correctness locks, queues, caches and pacing.
  - The live window of a remote run's job: calls, progress, notes, reasoning and visuals. Everything still arrives with the synced chat.
  - Display shortening and the small model's prompt shaping.
- Waits a removed timeout used to end now end on Stop, Cancel or Quit: Codex thread preparation, plugin startup and each MCP operation.
- Two removed limits had guarded against runaway loops: the context walk no longer recurses, and Codex model paging stops on a repeated cursor.
- Images attached while earlier ones are still being read now wait for them instead of being ignored.
- Rust no longer sends `limited` on file changes and workflow runs, so both schemas read it as optional, and older replies keep theirs.
- Tests:
  - Rust: the cap tests now accept large input (over 200 messages, a 3 MB CLI line, 600 context entries, 700 hooks, 250 plugins).
  - Unit: the tests that refused oversized input now accept it.
  - Browser: `tests/federation.spec.ts` adds a model read whole through the relay, and its views test forces a refused read. `screens.spec.ts`, `compaction.spec.ts`, `claude-fast-fallback.spec.ts`, `claude-settings.spec.ts`, `input-templates.spec.ts`, `model-viewer.spec.ts` and `workspace.spec.ts` follow the removed limits.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors), 631 Vitest tests, the build, 345 browser tests, `cargo fmt --check`, clippy and 450 Rust tests passed. Logs: `artifacts/relay-memory/`.

## Copies of one reply no longer split a chat into conflict copies — 2026-10-09

Requested 2026-10-09: chats kept gaining "(conflict copy)" versions, which should not happen.

- Cause, from the user's workspace: six copies since 2026-09-25, five in History and the sixth the chat still in use. Replaying `sameRun`'s checks on each copy and its original named what refused them. In five, one side holds a reply at an earlier step saved as "This response was interrupted when the app closed.", the mark a restart puts on a reply still running there, and before 9aab67c (on `development`, not yet released) every Viewer reload too, while the other side holds it finished. Two of those failed because `sameRun` reconciled copies of one reply only when one answer extended the other, and a reply's answer starts again after each call, its earlier text kept as a progress comment. One met an account the desktop switched to after a usage limit, which `sameRun` refused outright, and two no longer show what refused them. The sixth, this morning, came from a Viewer reopened with a checkpoint older than its saved copy: a turn it had taken in after the checkpoint counted as its own edit, and the desktop had rewound and resent that turn meanwhile.
- `laterReply` in `src/lib/sync.ts` keeps the later copy of a reply: its status first, then the blocks and usage revision it recorded, then an answer extending the other, otherwise the relay's. An account switch merges like any one-sided next-reply setting; two different switches still conflict.
- `onlyRepliesMoved` tells replies moving on from any other change. The incremental and whole-state polls write the checkpoint at once after taking in any other change, and a Viewer also after publishing one of its own; replies' progress keeps the one-minute interval.
- Unit: `sync.test.ts` adds the answer that started over (four orders of an earlier, a later and a running copy, an interrupted one, and two copies at the same step), the account switch beside a reply another device saw end, and the classification. On the previous `sync.ts` the first two made two conversations each. `transport.test.ts` checks that a message and an archive taken in write the checkpoint at once, while a reply's progress and this computer's own change wait.
- Browser: `tests/viewer-sync-conflicts.spec.ts` signs a Viewer in to a real relay and lets it take in a turn, its failure and another reply's progress. With the Viewer closed, the computer rewinds and resends the turn and the other reply answers anew. Reopened on the previous build, the Viewer left "Rewound on the computer (conflict copy)" and "Answer started over (conflict copy)" on the relay; now it leaves neither.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors), 631 Vitest tests, the build, 344 browser tests in three shards and `cargo fmt --check` passed. Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun because no Rust file changed. Logs: `artifacts/conflict-copies-fix/`.
- Not exercised: the installed app and the phone, which run the published release until the next one. Copies made before stay until deleted.

## Work history collapses from its end — 2026-10-08

Requested 2026-10-08: a long Work history should close from its end, without scrolling back to its start.

- Expanded Work history now ends with a divider that starts with **Collapse** (`.history-end` in `ToolActivity.svelte`), drawn like the summary; it replaces the disclosure's bottom border. It closes the disclosure, and focus it had moves to the summary without scrolling.
- `virtual-space.ts` holds a `data-collapse` control like any disclosure control. When the collapse removes both the content at the top of the view and the control, the disclosure's summary goes where the control was, so the Work history row ends up under the pointer. A history whose start was still in view keeps it there, as its summary does.
- Browser: `chat-virtual-space.spec.ts` adds a desktop and a phone scenario. A 40-step history read to the end of the conversation, and one in the middle of it with its control 300px down, both leave their summary within a pixel of the control's place, the second without held space; a short history whose summary was 150px down keeps it there. With the hand-off removed, the summary landed 1,419px away and the view showed only blank held space. `work-history.spec.ts` checks the divider and its control instead of the border, and that Enter on Collapse closes the history and focuses its summary.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors), 611 Vitest tests, the build, 339 browser tests (two shards of 170 and 169) and `cargo fmt --check` passed. Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun because no Rust file changed. Logs: `artifacts/work-history-collapse-end/`.
