## Move to history stays in the chat's project — 2026-10-07

Requested 2026-10-07: closing the last chat of a project should show an empty chat with that project selected instead of opening another project's chat.

- `nextActiveConversation` in `src/lib/locations.ts` now looks only in the closed chat's folder: the chat listed below it there, else the one above. Without one, `archiveConversation` in `+page.svelte` opens a new chat in that folder through `newChat` with `reveal` off, so the sidebar keeps its search and the toolbar and row actions still place focus themselves (the composer, the actions menu on phones, or the row now in its place). Standalone is a folder like the others, and a WSL folder that Desktop ran opens inside its distribution, as the folder's New conversation does. Deleting the open chat is unchanged: it opens a new chat in the folder chosen last.
- Unit: `locations.test.ts` checks that the next chat stays in its folder, never the folder or computer listed after it, and that a folder's last chat has none.
- Browser: `tests/move-to-history.spec.ts` adds two projects in the mock's `C:\Projects`. A project's bottom chat gives way to the one above it rather than to the next project's chat. Its last chat, from its row and from the toolbar, leaves a new chat with the project selected, focus on the next row or in the composer, and a message sent there runs in that folder. While searching, the project's chat outside the search comes before another project's match, and the new chat keeps the search. Both scenarios failed on the previous code, which opened the other project's chat.
- Pipeline: `npm run check` (0 errors), 611 Vitest tests, the build and `cargo fmt --check` passed. The first browser run passed 335 of 337: two local budgets in `performance.spec.ts` were missed under full-suite load (28.8 ms against 25 ms per update of an open sub-agent conversation, and a 184 ms task against 150 ms with 3,000 calls). That spec then passed alone (9 of 9), and the full rerun passed all 337. Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun because no Rust file changed. Logs: `artifacts/move-to-history-project/`.
- Not exercised: the native app. The change is frontend-only, and the scenarios ran in Edge, the engine of WebView2.

## WSL stops, WSL routing and transcript cleanup — 2026-10-07

Requested 2026-10-07 after the review comparing Agent Studio with the Claude app: a stop in WSL should end what the chat started, every new chat on a WSL folder should run inside the distribution, and Claude Code's 30-day cleanup should not break chats.

- Transcript cleanup: Claude Code 2.1.292's own code shows the sweep (`cleanupPeriodDays`, 30 by default, once a day per directory through `.last-cleanup`) and that only desktop-host transcripts are exempt; every Agent Studio profile here held a recent `.last-cleanup`, so headless sessions sweep too. `retention::tests` cover the inserted key (after the brace, CRLF and compact files, an empty object), a period the user set, comments, a byte order mark, non-objects, a link, a read-only file, a missing file and the kept mode. `share_retain` ran in Ubuntu against throwaway folders with the same cases (13 checks, including the new file's mode under umask 022 and no stray temporary files). `providers::tests` check that chat and restricted launches carry the period and restricted ones nothing else. `sessions::tests::a_claude_session_whose_transcript_was_cleaned_up_starts_over_from_the_saved_messages` and `a_claude_transcript_is_found_by_its_folder_name_or_by_looking_through_every_folder` cover a missing projects folder (resume as before), the folder name and the full scan, Claude Code's hashed long names, Codex and copied account switches left alone, and the new binding.
- Real CLI: the opt-in `installed_claude_continues_a_chat_whose_transcript_was_cleaned_up` passed twice with the installed Claude Code 2.1.292 and Haiku. A first reply saved a transcript and the next request still resumed it; with the transcript deleted, the next reply started a new session from the saved messages, answered the codeword from the first message (KESTREL-2093) and the binding kept the new session. Its project folder is removed afterwards.
- WSL stops: in Ubuntu, a session started by a `wsl.exe` call survives that call only once it is in a session of its own, which is how Claude Code runs shell commands, so the old process-group stop left such commands running. The opt-in `real_wsl_bridge_preserves_stdin_isolates_profiles_and_kills_descendants` now starts, under a fixture CLI, a command in its group, one in its own session and one orphaned to init, checks all four run, stops the launch and checks all four ended while an unrelated `sleep` of the same user kept running. With the kill reduced to the old group signal the same test failed on the detached command. `a_real_wsl_profile_links_the_shared_claude_directory_before_its_cli_starts` also checks that the CLI inherits its `AGENT_STUDIO_JOB` and that the shared `settings.json` gained the period.
- WSL routing: `locations.test.ts` covers `\\wsl.localhost`, `\\wsl$`, forward-slash and upper-case paths into Ubuntu, the distribution root, another distribution, a network share and a drive path, and a scratch chat saved with Desktop running the folder. The browser test `a WSL folder chosen on Desktop runs inside its distribution…` now also starts a new chat from the folder an older Desktop chat ran in and checks it runs in Ubuntu without an execution override.
- Pipeline: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test` (440 passed, 30 opt-in ignored), `npm run check`, 612 Vitest tests, the build and 335 browser tests passed. The first browser run failed once in `sent-files.spec.ts` on a label that matched both the closing model preview and its Close button; it passed alone three times and now matches the label exactly.
- Not exercised: a stop of a real Claude reply inside WSL, since the WSL Claude profiles here are not signed in; a built app adding the period to the real `~/.claude/settings.json`; native macOS and Linux stops, which still end only the CLI's process group.

## Windows shares folders without permission and asks once for the two files — 2026-10-06

Requested 2026-10-06: instead of requiring Developer Mode, Agent Studio should ask Windows' permission once and keep running as the user.

- Windows allows junctions and hard links without any permission, so both were tried with the installed Claude Code 2.1.291 in throwaway folders. A plugin installed through a junctioned `plugins` folder landed in the shared folder, and the junction stayed. A hard-linked `settings.json` broke as soon as Claude Code saved it, on Windows and in Ubuntu alike: Claude Code writes a new file and swaps it in, so the shared file never got the change. Symbolic links survive because Claude Code follows them before saving. So folders became junctions, and only the two files need symbolic links.
- Unit tests (no permission): `linking::tests::folders_are_shared_without_any_permission_and_relinked_when_moved` created real junctions on this Windows computer, wrote through them, repaired one pointing elsewhere and removed one without touching the shared folder. `files_are_linked_when_this_process_may_link_them` and `only_the_first_attempt_of_a_run_moves_folders` cover the case without link rights: nothing moves, and the state reads as waiting for permission. `the_elevated_helper_links_only_inside_this_apps_profiles` and `the_elevated_helper_accepts_only_its_own_arguments` check that the helper refuses a source outside the user's folder, a profile folder that is itself a link, malformed ids and extra arguments. `arguments_are_quoted_as_windows_splits_them` checks the helper's command line.
- Real elevation: the opt-in `windows_permission_links_both_files_and_claude_code_writes_through_them` passed in 4.1 s, once the user approved the Windows prompt. The built `agent-studio.exe` ran as the elevated helper and linked both files of a throwaway profile, with a random id in this app's data, to a throwaway directory in the user's `.cache`. Claude Code, running unelevated, then installed a fixture plugin through the profile: `enabledPlugins` reached the shared `settings.json`, `cleanupPeriodDays` stayed, and both links remained links. Both folders were removed afterwards. The first attempt passed a relative path to the helper, which Windows could not find from its system folder; `request_permission` now makes the path absolute.
- Browser: `claude-sharing.spec.ts` covers a new separate Claude account showing the note, a declined Allow with its error, and an Allow that clears the note, asking Windows once per click.
- Pipeline: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test` (433 passed, 29 opt-in ignored), `npm run check` (0 errors), 612 Vitest tests, the build and 335 browser tests passed.
- Not exercised: the automatic prompt on a real first start, and the move of real Windows profiles into `C:\Users\…\.claude` in a built app.

## Claude accounts share the Claude app's directory, and sign in themselves in WSL — 2026-10-06

Requested 2026-10-06: Agent Studio and the Claude app should be interchangeable, with memories, configuration, MCP servers and everything else configured in WSL available and maintained in both, and WSL Claude accounts should sign in once each instead of borrowing the Windows login. Claude Code 2.1.291 and Codex 0.160.1 on Windows and in Ubuntu (WSL 2).

- Unit tests: `linking::tests` cover moving new items, merging folders, keeping differing copies in `before-sharing`, dropping identical ones and never replacing anything; that only the first attempt of a run moves files; and that a profile never links to its own directory. The test that creates real links skips itself on a computer without link rights, and did here, because Developer Mode was off. Also: `wsl::tests::a_wsl_launch_keeps_one_reusable_identity_and_flags_shared_profiles`, the import source listing in `imports::tests` (a linked profile is listed once), `profiles::tests` (only Codex profiles borrow), `lending::tests` (Codex only) and `fleet.test.ts`. In the browser, `wsl-setup.spec.ts` now covers a Desktop Claude account that joins Ubuntu, asks to sign in there, signs in inside Ubuntu, and keeps its own login when the Windows one signs out.
- Real WSL: the share script ran on a throwaway profile holding its own transcript, a conflicting `MEMORY.md`, an identical memory file, a conflicting `settings.json`, a conflicting synced skill and a `CLAUDE.md`, linked to a throwaway Claude directory. All 13 items became links, and new transcripts moved into the shared directory. The shared memory and settings stayed, and the differing copies went to `before-sharing`. The identical file was dropped, `CLAUDE.md` was adopted, and `.credentials.json` and `.claude.json` stayed in the profile. A second run and the repair of a link pointing elsewhere were both no-ops.
- The opt-in `a_real_wsl_profile_links_the_shared_claude_directory_before_its_cli_starts` passed in 0.45 s. The real launch linked a profile to a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` passed through `WSLENV`, and the CLI read its moved transcript through the link with the user's umask (no longer 0077). `real_wsl_bridge_preserves_stdin_isolates_profiles_and_kills_descendants` still passes.
- Claude Code writes through a linked `settings.json`. In a throwaway profile whose `settings.json` linked to a shared file, `claude plugin marketplace add` and `claude plugin install … --scope user` wrote `extraKnownMarketplaces` and `enabledPlugins` into the shared file, kept its existing keys, and left the link in place.
- Windows cannot follow a link inside WSL through `\\wsl.localhost`, whether its target is absolute or relative: reading through one gives ENOENT, and lstat reports EISDIR. The host therefore reads a linked profile's files from the shared directory.
- The Claude app's WSL sessions run `~/.claude/remote/ccd-cli` with `--permission-mode bypassPermissions --setting-sources=user,project,local`, without `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. They use the distribution's own `~/.claude`, the directory linked WSL profiles now share.
- Pipeline: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, and `cargo test` (429 passed, 28 opt-in ignored) passed, as did `npm run check` (0 errors), 612 Vitest tests and the build. The final browser run passed all 334 tests. Earlier runs on this busy computer had timing failures that passed when rerun. `performance.spec.ts` "a reply with thousands of calls" missed its local 150 ms budget at 163–168 ms three times, then passed four reruns, two with these changes and two without them. A mobile Files edited alignment check and a phone alert poll each failed once.
- Not exercised at the time: links on Windows, because Developer Mode was off (covered in the next entry). That leaves untested the migration of real Windows profiles into `C:\Users\…\.claude`, a real reply on a linked profile, an account switch resuming in place, adding an MCP server or installing a plugin into the shared directory, and a WSL Claude sign-in finished in a browser.

## Claude Code and Codex sign in without a terminal — 2026-10-06

The user asked whether Open sign-in could skip the terminal and open only the OAuth page. Probes of the installed Claude Code 2.1.291 and Codex 0.160.1 in throwaway profiles, with a `BROWSER` that names no program so that no page opened, showed that both can. `claude auth login` runs as a hidden process with piped standard handles. It prints its fallback page, which redirects to `platform.claude.com/oauth/code/callback` and ends with a code to paste, opens the page that returns to a local port itself (through `BROWSER` when set, otherwise `rundll32 url,OpenURL`), and takes `code#state` lines on its input, refusing malformed ones without ending. It exits 0 after "Login successful." or 1 with "Login failed: …"; a wrong code gives "Request failed with status code 400". Codex's app-server answers `account/login/start {type:"chatgpt"}` with a `loginId` and an `authUrl` returning to `127.0.0.1:1455`, which the client opens, and reports `account/login/completed`; `account/login/cancel` ends it.

`src-tauri/src/sign_in.rs` runs those for the selected profile (the lender's profile for a WSL account that borrows a Windows login). It returns a sign-in once its page is known, opens the page itself for Codex, and for a distribution's own Claude login, whose Linux CLI cannot reach the Windows browser (Ubuntu here has no `xdg-open`), passes `BROWSER=true` through `WSLENV`. A pasted code goes to the CLI only when it is shaped like one and repeats the state of this sign-in's page. A new sign-in of the same login ends a waiting one, and a Codex sign-in ends any other on the computer, because its page returns to one fixed port. Cancel and Quit end the owned process; a sign-in nobody finishes waits. Windows follow sign-ins through `studio-sign-in` events and read waiting ones again after a reload (`sign_ins`). In Connections a waiting sign-in replaces Open sign-in with **Open sign-in page** and **Cancel**. Claude's code field opens once its page is opened from the app, on **Page shows a code?** (focused), or at once for a WSL login. A sign-in that ends badly shows the CLI's reason on the account. An account reads connected only once its CLI reported success and its check reports ready. Antigravity, which signs in inside its own interactive screen, keeps its terminal.

Validation: `npm run check` (0 diagnostics in 5,223 files), 612 unit tests in 81 files, production build, 334 browser scenarios in two shards of 167, Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 425 Rust tests with 28 opt-in cases ignored. Logs: `artifacts/sign-in-browser/`. New coverage:

- Rust tests drive Node stand-ins for both CLIs: Claude signs in hidden, opens its own page, refuses a code without `#` or from another sign-in before the CLI sees it, and connects on a good one; a refused code ends with the CLI's reason; a CLI that cannot start says why; Codex opens its page, declines the app-server's own requests, ignores another login's completion and connects or fails with its error; a page that did not open says so; Codex replaces another Codex sign-in, Cancel ends one, and a sign-in nobody finishes waits until it is cancelled. Unit tests cover pages (whole https lines only, terminal links stripped, no credentials or other schemes), pasted codes and CLI reasons.
- `src/lib/sign-in.test.ts`: which updates a window shows, checks, clears or ignores, and the bounded schema.
- `tests/account-sign-in.spec.ts`: the waiting sign-in in place of Open sign-in, its page opened again, a code from another sign-in refused, the code field and Checking the code…, a refused code's reason on the account and in the notice, a waiting sign-in restored after a reload, Cancel, and a finished sign-in connecting the account; the earlier sign-in scenarios now finish their sign-ins as the CLI reports them. The desktop mock keeps waiting sign-ins (`test-sign-ins`), ends one with `finishSignIn(key, phase, message)` and delivers native events through `emitTauriEvent`.

Real checks on Windows 11, with no browser opened and no account signed in:

- Opt-in `installed_clis_show_their_sign_in_pages_without_a_terminal` (0.7 s: both installed CLIs in throwaway profiles show their pages through the new code and stop on Cancel) and `wsl_sign_ins_open_their_page_on_windows_and_stop_inside_the_distribution` (2 s, Ubuntu: `BROWSER` reaches the CLI through `WSLENV`, Claude Code and Codex inside the distribution show their pages, which the app opens on Windows, and nothing that ran with the throwaway profile still runs after Cancel). Run them with the `CLAUDE*` variables of a Claude session removed.
- `node scripts/sign-in-native-smoke.mjs` against a QA build (`scripts/native-sign-in.tauri.json`, CDP 19821), started from a shell outside MSIX so that `BROWSER` reaches Claude Code: Add account started `claude.exe auth login` with no window and no terminal process under the app, the code field opened focused, a code from another sign-in never reached the CLI, a well-formed code went to the real CLI, which Anthropic's token endpoint refused (shown as "Claude did not accept the code (Request failed with status code 400). Open sign-in to try again."), a new sign-in survived a reload of the window, and Cancel ended the CLI. Screenshots: `artifacts/sign-in-native/`.

Not exercised: completing a real sign-in in a browser, a Windows browser returning to a Codex sign-in inside WSL (that relies on WSL's localhost forwarding, on by default, to reach `127.0.0.1:1455` in the distribution), macOS and Linux hosts, where Claude Code opens the page with the system opener as it did in the terminal, and Antigravity, which is not installed here (its terminal is unchanged).

## Running Claude replies report their context — 2026-10-02

The user noticed that the Context bar sometimes read **Not measured yet**, with a screenshot of a first reply 16 minutes and dozens of commands into its turn. The Claude decoder kept each request's input as it arrived but sent it to the page only with the turn's `result`, so until a turn ended the meter had nothing: a chat's first reply showed no context for its whole run, a later reply showed the previous reply's figure, and a reply that never reached its result kept none. The saved workspace showed all three. Two replies running at the time had no reading at all, one of them 316 calls in, and four replies interrupted when the app closed, one after 65 calls, had none afterwards; every completed Claude reply had one. Codex already reported `thread/tokenUsage/updated` during its turns.

The decoder now sends a `usage` event whenever the reply's latest parent request reports a new count: from its `message_start` stream event, which in Claude Code 2.1.287 carries the request's `input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens` and its model before any block (a long thinking block otherwise holds the reading back), or from its assistant lines, which repeat it. The event keeps the counts and cost of the reply's latest result (none before the first) and drops the window measured for another model, such as a fallback. A sub-agent's requests, Claude Code's own `<synthetic>` messages and repeats of the same count send nothing, and after a compaction, which clears the page's reading, the next request reports its count whatever it reads. The page already used a running reply's `contextInput`, so a running reply now shows its own context, and a reply stopped or cut off before its result keeps its last. Imports keep only their own reading, since `coalesce` replaces the decoder's with it.

Validation: `npm run check` (0 diagnostics in 5,214 files), 602 unit tests in 79 files, production build, 330 browser scenarios in two shards of 165, Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 400 Rust tests with 23 opt-in cases ignored. While other replies ran on the machine, two runs of the second shard each missed one timing check this change does not touch: the 25 ms budget of `an open sub-agent conversation of 300 messages stays responsive while it streams`, by 0.04 ms, and the 5-second wait for the phone's next alert in `a phone takes back alerts of chats read elsewhere with its next alert and when the Viewer opens`. Each passed alone (the second three times), and the shard then passed whole. New coverage:

- Rust test `claude_reports_each_requests_context_while_its_turn_runs`: a reading at the first `message_start` without counts, cost or window, none for repeated blocks, a sub-agent's request or a start without usage, the next request's reading, a later turn's request keeping the result's counts, cost and window, none for Claude Code's own message, a reading after a compaction even at the same count, and a fallback model's request without the first model's window.
- Unit test: a first reply's reading against the usage snapshot's window while it runs, kept once it is stopped.
- Opt-in `installed_claude_reports_its_context_before_the_turn_ends` against the installed Claude Code with this session's profile (4.1 s): readings of 29,293 tokens before the command and 31,271 before the result, which reported the same 31,271 with 60,564 input tokens for the turn.

## Code blocks copy, and shell blocks run in a console — 2026-10-02

The user asked that code in a reply offer two things: copy it, and run it on a console. Every fenced block in rendered Markdown (replies, progress comments, reasoning, proposed plans, a sub-agent's messages) now has **Copy** in its top right corner, and blocks labelled for a console (`bash`, `sh`, `shell`, `zsh`; `powershell`, `pwsh`, `ps1`, `ps`; `cmd`, `bat`, `batch`, `dos`) have **Run** beside it. The controls are quiet until the block is hovered or focused, and always shown in a row above the code on touch screens and phones. A fence still being written has none. `markdown.ts` renders each block after sanitization, in place of a per-window mark its fence leaves (`withCodeBlocks`), so the sanitizer still allows neither `<div>` nor `<button>` and markup a reply writes cannot become a control or alter a block's code; `src/lib/code-blocks.ts` names the console languages, decides where a console can open (`consoleShells`) and handles a click, once, from the window's capture phase. Run calls `run_in_console` (`src-tauri/src/console.rs`), which resolves the chat's folder on the host as a reply does (`mcp::working_folder`, split from `mcp::folder` so it needs no provider CLI), writes the code to a file and opens a console window that reads it: PowerShell (in the session itself), Command Prompt (a batch file), Git Bash on Windows, the distribution for a WSL chat, or a terminal on macOS and Linux. POSIX shells go through `console-launch.sh`, which runs the code in an interactive login shell and starts that shell again when the code ends. The sign-in console and these consoles now share one launcher (`console/window.rs`). Run is offered only in the desktop window of the chat's own computer and has no confirmation; other computers' chats and the Viewer only copy. Details and limits are in docs/CAPABILITIES.md.

Three things changed during the work because the real consoles showed them. `wsl.exe` reads its own options with their quotes, so `--distribution "Ubuntu"` names no distribution: the name is passed bare and names outside letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-` are refused. A shell's own settings can change folder (a `cd ~` in `.bashrc`), so the launchers return to the chat's folder after those settings load and run nothing when that folder is unavailable; Command Prompt, which silently falls back to the Windows folder for a network path, refuses such a chat. And an Ubuntu profile that makes `PROMPT_COMMAND` an array does not export it, so the WSL test reads the final shell from `/proc` instead.

Validation: `npm run check` (0 diagnostics in 5,214 files), 601 unit/HTTP tests in 79 files, production build, 330 browser scenarios in two shards of 165, Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 399 Rust tests with 22 opt-in cases ignored. New coverage:

- Unit tests: the console languages (including that `constructor` is not one), closed and open fences, and where a console can open (Windows, a WSL distribution, macOS and Linux, another computer, the Viewer, a chat without a connection).
- Rust tests: bounded code and each kind of hidden character, the three shells a request may name, window titles, pruning of kept code, Windows argument quoting and the environment block, that the launcher only names the code, Git Bash found beside Git and never as the WSL launcher, and Command Prompt refused in a network folder with nothing kept.
- `tests/code-blocks.spec.ts`: controls appear only when a fence closes; five blocks copy and three run; hover and keyboard focus; exact code for a block nested in a list; **Copied**, **Not copied**, **Opening…**, **Opened in …** and the note of a console that could not open; the request names the chat, its folder and the block's shell; one console per click; phone layout; saved history; forged `<div>`/`<button>` markup and a link around a block; a WSL chat offers only POSIX blocks, a chat another computer runs offers none; and the Viewer's production build copies and never offers a console. Selecting a reply does not take the controls' labels.

Real checks on Windows 11, with no provider involved. The opt-in `real_windows_consoles_run_the_code_in_the_folder_and_stay_open` and `a_real_wsl_console_runs_the_code_in_the_folder_and_stays_open` open PowerShell, Command Prompt, Git Bash and an Ubuntu console without windows: each ran its code in a folder named `Project 'quoted' & (odd) ñ` (`/tmp/studio … 'quoted' $(literal) ñ` in Ubuntu), stayed open afterwards with what the code defined or exported, never saw the console's own variables or `CLAUDECODE`, and left no code behind; a batch file went when its Command Prompt closed. `scripts/console-native-smoke.mjs` then clicked Run in an isolated build (`com.vinicius.agentstudio.console-qa`, `scripts/native-console.tauri.json`, CDP 19771) and read each real console's screen: the code first, its output, then a live prompt, in PowerShell, Command Prompt, Git Bash and Ubuntu; a block holding a right-to-left override was refused with its code point, and a chat whose folder was missing opened nothing. The sign-in console's own opt-in test still passes through the shared launcher. `console-launch.sh` was also run under a pseudo-terminal in Ubuntu: `exit` and `set -e` end the code and leave the shell, exports and the folder carry over, a shell whose settings change folder still runs the code in the chat's folder, and a missing folder runs nothing. Report and screenshot: `artifacts/console/`.

Not exercised: macOS and Linux terminals (that code path is type-checked against the Linux standard library, and its launcher script is the one tested in Ubuntu), PowerShell 7 (`pwsh` is not installed here), and a Windows computer without Git Bash, where a POSIX block opens in PowerShell.

## Phone alerts of chats read elsewhere close themselves — 2026-10-02

The user asked whether opening a chat could remove its push notification, so the phone stops showing alerts for chats already read. Their phone is an iPhone. There, three pushes that show nothing cancel the subscription (WebKit's silent push quota), so the relay cannot wake the phone only to remove an alert. `notification.close()` works on iOS since 18 ([WebKit 248309](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=248309)), and iOS still does not replace an alert of the same tag ([WebKit 258922](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=258922)). Reading a chat now makes its alerts stale, as does deleting it; reading means a visible, focused view on any device, the signal that already suppresses pushes. The phone closes stale alerts the next time it runs. `relay/push.ts` records in `web-push.json` which chats each subscriber was alerted about (`shown`: the newest send and, after a reading, its time; a week and 64 chats per device). It stamps each push with the relay's `sentAt` and adds the stale chats with their reading times (`close`, the 20 newest) to every later push to that device, until its Viewer page takes them from the `/v1/notification-view` answer. Every push repeats them because a push service replaces an alert it still holds with a later one of the same topic. The worker shows its alert first, then closes the alerts of those chats sent up to the reading, and older alerts of its own tag. The page closes the alerts of the chat it opens and those the relay hands it (`closeNotifications`, `readSince` and `staleAlerts` in `src/lib/notifications.ts`). Every push still shows an alert.

Validation: `npm run check` (0 diagnostics in 5,211 files), 594 unit/HTTP tests in 78 files, production build, 326 browser scenarios in two shards of 163, and Rust formatting. Two performance budgets missed under parallel load (25.6 ms against 25, and 175 ms against 150); all nine performance scenarios passed when rerun alone. Clippy and the Rust tests were not rerun because no Rust file changed. New coverage:

- Relay unit tests: the stale alert goes out with the next alert and repeats until the page takes it; it survives a restart; an alert newer than its chat's reading stays; deletion; the week and per-device bounds; key rotation.
- An HTTP test of the notification-view answer for a paired browser and for a computer.
- A worker test whose fake registration keeps alerts of the same tag side by side, as iOS does.
- A browser scenario with the compiled worker: a chat read on a computer loses its alert when the phone's next alert arrives; another is closed when the Viewer opens; opening chats in the Viewer closes theirs, including an alert the relay never recorded.

Removing any one of these steps fails the tests: the record before sending, the deletion marking, the page hand-off, the page's own close, or the worker's close by tag or by reading. Not exercised: a physical iPhone or Android phone and real push services. Chromium replaces same-tag alerts itself, so only the unit test covers the worker closing them. Logs: `artifacts/stale-alerts/`.

## A small model chooses each project folder's icon — 2026-10-02

After a new conversation's title request, `chooseFolderIcon` asks the executing host (`generate_folder_icon`, the strictly validated `folderIcon` relay job from other devices) when the folder has no icon. `src-tauri/src/folder_icons.rs` builds the prompt from the same catalog and runs it through the title path, now shared as `titles::background_request` and `runner::background_text`: the provider's title model, no tools, the title runtime, 30 seconds, two at a time per computer.
