## Replies wait for declared background work — 2026-09-22

Claude chats announced completion whenever Claude ended a turn to wait for tests it had started with `run_in_background`, and the follow-up turn the CLI ran after the tests exited went into the parked process, where its output was discarded. Counting background tasks cannot fix this because a dev server left for the user is also a running task, and Claude Code's own session state ignores background shells for the same reason. Claude chats now expose the always-loaded `await_background_tasks` SDK tool. The model declares the finite tasks it waits for; the reply stays open until each declared outcome reaches the model and ends after that turn, while undeclared servers never hold it. Stop during a wait stops only the declared and delegated tasks and parks the process. Multi-turn replies now sum their token counts. See docs/NATIVE-SESSIONS.md.

Validation: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 276 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 193 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and the full Rust suite (253 tests, eight opt-in cases ignored). The frontend is unchanged; the first browser run in the fresh worktree failed one updater scenario on a cold Vite module load, and the rerun passed. New coverage: tool validation and ownership (parent registration, child tasks, foreground commands, finished, unknown and malformed IDs), delivery through replayed notifications or a follow-up turn, stopped tasks, the always-loaded read-only definition, multi-turn token totals, and controlled PowerShell children for the ordinary wait, a completion before the result, a replayed completion, the fallback when no follow-up turn arrives, and Stop while idle. Real Claude Code 2.1.278 checks with Sonnet: a protocol probe showed the model declaring only its test task beside a stand-in server, and the two opt-in `installed_claude_*` tests passed through the production runner (a 29-second wait for a 20-second test command with the server left running, and Stop ending a waiting reply with a parkable process). No native app UI run, WSL, macOS/Linux, or Viewer-started reply was exercised.

## Signed automatic updates from GitHub Releases — 2026-09-22

Installed desktop apps previously had no way to update themselves; new versions required downloading an installer from an Actions run. The app now uses the Tauri updater with this repository's GitHub Releases. Release installations (NSIS/MSI, AppImage, macOS app) check `releases/latest/download/latest.json` 30 seconds after startup and every six hours, download a newer package in the background, and verify its minisign signature and signed version (`requireSignedVersion`) before offering **Restart to update** in the sidebar footer and in Settings. Restarting waits for replies running on this computer, saves the workspace, and releases owned CLI work like the close button before the installer relaunches the app; closing an idle app installs without relaunching. Merges to `main` publish `v<version>` releases only after verification, every platform build, and the release script's signature/version checks pass, and never replace a published release. The app moved to 0.2.0 and the Tauri CLI to 2.11.5, the first version that signs the release version. See docs/UPDATES.md.

Validation on the final candidate: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 276 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 193 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 244 Rust tests with six existing opt-in cases ignored. New coverage: Rust availability by bundle type, the install gate, bounded error mapping, release-note sanitizing and status serialization; frontend status parsing and summaries; browser scenarios for the disabled-then-enabled sidebar restart, saving before installation, a refused restart, Settings states and release notes, and no update controls in the web viewer; and release-script tests for version bumps across all five manifests with both line endings, real Tauri CLI signatures including tampered data and trusted comments, and release staging. Native Windows verification with `scripts/updates-native-smoke.mjs` built three signed NSIS releases (0.2.0–0.2.2) of the isolated **Agent Studio Update QA** identity (separate identifier, product name, executable name and throwaway key), installed 0.2.0 through an unpackaged task, and confirmed: the background check downloaded and verified 0.2.1 and offered Restart to update; restarting installed 0.2.1 through the passive installer, with no SmartScreen prompt, and relaunched it; a manifest announcing 0.2.9 with the 0.2.2 package and signature was rejected with the signature-verification message; and closing the idle app installed 0.2.2 without reopening it. The QA app was uninstalled and its data removed; the regular Agent Studio 0.1.0 installation and its running process were untouched. Screenshots of the sidebar and Settings were reviewed; the footer button takes its own row so it fits the default sidebar width. macOS and Linux updates, the MSI path, and the real GitHub endpoint were not exercised; the first release publishes when this change merges to `main` with the signing secrets configured.

## Terminal logins can become separate profiles — 2026-09-21

Manage account showed different shared-context choices for the two connection kinds: separate profiles offered **This computer's CLI context** and **This account only**, while a detected terminal login offered only **This account only**, which read as an opt-out although that login is the computer context by itself. Every Claude/Codex connection now offers both choices. A terminal login shows the computer context selected; choosing This account only converts that connection into a separate profile for the same account: the connection keeps its id, becomes `profile: "isolated"` with `sharedContext: "none"` and a `fromTerminalLogin` marker, its status resets to sign-in and the sign-in opens automatically, and the terminal directory stays signed in and untouched. Native session bindings already treat the changed profile root as an account switch; history transfer now also tries the connection's terminal-login variant as a source, so chats bound to the converted connection continue from the terminal's transcript. Detection treats a marked sibling as the terminal login's own account when the CLI reports no identity, so Codex's terminal login is not registered again as another label after conversion.

Validation on the final candidate: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 261 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 187 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 238 Rust tests with six existing opt-in cases ignored. New coverage: the Rust binding detects the same connection with a new profile root as a switch and the terminal-login variant reproduces the previous scope; the frontend choice helpers convert a terminal login and keep it representable in the schema; detection skips a marked sibling without an identity; and a browser scenario converts the Codex terminal login, sees the sign-in launch, and confirms a refresh adds no second label. A live conversion with a real sign-in and a transferred reply was not run for this change.

## Shared context includes MCP server definitions — 2026-09-21

Shared context previously covered instructions, rules, skills and memories but not MCP servers, so a separate profile started without the terminal's locally configured servers (on the reporting machine: Claude user-scope rustrover, rider and linear, plus a local-scope unreal server for one Unreal project; Codex node_repl, rustrover and rider). Shared context now also loads the source's MCP definitions. Claude receives user-scope servers and the selected folder's local-scope servers from the source's `.claude.json` through `--mcp-config`, merged with the app's own `agent_studio` server; the folder key follows the CLI's own conventions (forward slashes on Windows, `wsl:<distribution>:<path>` for WSL folders). Codex receives the source's `[mcp_servers.*]` tables flattened into `-c` overrides with escaped TOML literals. Context inspection passes the same definitions so the MCPs tab reports them for the selected profile. Only definitions travel; MCP sign-in state, every other configuration key and credentials are never read into the result, definitions are handed to the launched process only, and process-reuse identities carry bounded names plus a digest instead of bodies. Definitions above 64 servers or 24 KB fail with an explicit message.

Validation on the final candidate: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 260 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 186 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 237 Rust tests with six existing opt-in cases ignored. New Rust coverage: Claude scope selection for Windows, WSL-UNC and Linux folders with sign-in state excluded and the reserved server name skipped, Codex table flattening with escaped strings, nested env tables and skipped non-bare names, and metadata serialization that carries names and a digest but no definition bodies. A live reply through a separate profile using shared servers was not run for this change; the launch flags are the CLI's documented `--mcp-config` and `-c` mechanisms already used by the app.

## Separate profiles default to the computer's CLI context — 2026-09-21

Separate account profiles created through Add account previously started with an empty configuration directory and shared nothing unless the user chose another account connection as a source. The reporting workspace had four Claude profiles pointing at another separate profile that held no instructions or memories, while the terminal's directory held the real context. Isolated connections now resolve **this computer's CLI context** as their default shared source: the host synthesizes the environment's default CLI profile (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, or the managed WSL home equivalent) without requiring a detected-login connection, and the existing shared-context path supplies its instructions, rules, skills, commands and project memory directory. `sharedContext: "none"` keeps a profile separate, an explicit account source still overrides, and detected terminal logins never default to sharing. Manage account offers **This computer’s CLI context** first for separate profiles; Model context labels those references **Computer context** and states when a shared source has nothing for the selected folder. Credentials, model settings, installed-plugin configuration and MCP authentication remain account-specific.

Validation on the final candidate: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 260 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 186 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 235 Rust tests with six existing opt-in cases ignored. New coverage: Rust source-kind resolution for isolated, opted-out, explicit and existing connections; the frontend choice helpers and schema round trip; and the shared-context browser scenario, which now expects the computer default, saves an explicit account source, opts out to This account only, and returns to the computer default. A real-provider check of a separate profile reading the terminal directory's memories was not repeated for this change; the shared-context path it reuses was verified natively when that feature landed, as recorded in docs/SHARED-CONTEXT.md.

## WSL scripts keep LF endings in installer builds — 2026-09-21

The installed Windows build reported **WSL · Ubuntu: WSL CLI inspection failed** and showed every Linux CLI as not found, while the same commands succeeded from a development checkout. The installer is compiled on a `windows-latest` runner whose checkout applies `core.autocrlf`, so the shell scripts embedded with `include_str!` carried CRLF endings; bash inside Ubuntu rejected them with exit 2 (`syntax error near unexpected token $'do\r'`). The repository had no `.gitattributes`, and only `wsl-cancel.sh` stripped CRLF at runtime. `.gitattributes` now pins `*.sh` to LF, `wsl::embedded_script` normalizes every embedded script (launch, cancel, CLI resolution, inventory, WSL folder listing, Windows path translation and context path discovery), and a Rust test fails when a checked-out script contains a carriage return.

Diagnosis on the reporting machine: the installed executable's bytes contained `\r\n` inside both embedded scripts; watching the running app's `wsl.exe` children during a refresh showed exit code 2, whereas a console-less probe with the app's creation flags, the System32 binary and the Store alias exited 0 with LF scripts; feeding the inventory script with CRLF endings reproduced the exact failure. Validation on the final candidate: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 259 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 186 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 234 Rust tests with six existing opt-in cases ignored. A rebuilt installer is required for the fix to reach installed apps; the currently installed 0.1.0 build still contains CRLF scripts.

## Identity-aware CLI login detection — 2026-09-21

Connections no longer adds a duplicate **Claude CLI login** label when the terminal's Claude login is the same account as a separate profile already connected on that computer. Provider and per-connection status now carries the identity the CLI itself reports (`claude auth status` email; Codex `login status` and Gemini report none) as session-only metadata, never persisted, synced or read from credential files. Existing-login registration compares that identity with the sibling connections on the same environment: a matching login is skipped, an unchecked login or sibling defers the decision while the agent is already offered there, a signed-out or identity-less login keeps today's behaviour, and a different signed-in account becomes the CLI login connection, reusing an unconnected default label rather than adding another. Managed WSL distributions are probed through a new read-only `detect_environment_login` command before any connection exists, and a WSL terminal login matching an account connected elsewhere on the computer is connected under that account's label. Nothing is removed or relabelled automatically; refresh checks connected accounts before deciding about terminal logins.

Validation on the final candidate: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 259 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 186 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 233 Rust tests with six existing opt-in cases ignored. New coverage: Rust parsing of the Claude status report (signed-out, missing, blank and oversized emails), unit rules for waiting, matching, unresolved siblings, different accounts, label reuse and cross-environment label adoption, and a browser scenario that removes the detected login beside a matching separate profile, refreshes, reloads, sees only the remaining profile in the Agent picker, then watches the login return under its old label once the terminal reports a different account.

Real Windows check: the installed Claude Code CLI reported the default login email and each isolated profile's email through `claude auth status` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; the saved workspace of the reporting installation contained four isolated Claude profiles plus a re-added **Claude CLI login** whose identity matched one of them, which this change stops from being recreated after removal. Codex CLI 0.153.4 `login status` prints no identity, so Codex terminal logins keep the previous behaviour. Native WSL probing, macOS/Linux desktop and physical remote hosts were not live-tested; the already-saved duplicate connection is left for the user to remove once.

## MCP management and OAuth — 2026-09-19

Model context's MCPs tab now manages the selected CLI profile through local or authenticated relay transport. It supports saved server addition, explicit OAuth sign-in/cancellation and sign-out, Claude live server replacement/reconnect/toggle, and Codex reload/startup status. OAuth waits for provider confirmation; a returned authorization URL is not success. Claude live controls require the exact idle parked conversation, preserve the Agent Studio SDK server, and never interrupt a running reply. Inspection remains passive. Authorization links, callbacks and management input remain transient and are excluded from workspace/history/context persistence.

## Persistent conversation processes — 2026-09-19

Claude and Codex chats now keep one CLI process per conversation between replies (`src-tauri/src/pool.rs`). A completed or cleanly interrupted reply parks the process; the next reply reuses it when the provider, account/profile scope, computer, folder, executable, model, reasoning, and provider-reported native session match, and otherwise restarts. Stop sends Claude's `interrupt` control request or Codex `turn/interrupt`, waits up to five seconds for the turn to end, and keeps the process; an unconfirmed interrupt still kills the owned tree. The composer queues up to eight messages per conversation while a reply runs, sends the next one after a completed reply, and returns them to the draft after a stopped or failed reply.

All required checks passed: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 202 frontend/HTTP unit tests, production build, 134 browser scenarios including two new queue scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 141 Rust tests with four existing opt-in integrations ignored. New Rust coverage drives the real Claude and Codex turn loops with controlled PowerShell children: an interrupt that keeps the process and a second turn that reuses it, the kill fallback after the grace period, a second Codex turn on the parked thread, pool eviction and the four-process bound, idle expiry under the virtual clock, and the reuse identity check against a Codex-named thread.

Real-provider checks used Claude Code 2.1.277 (Sonnet, low effort) and Codex CLI 0.153.4 (GPT-5.6-Sol, low effort) with the existing Windows logins. `scripts/claude-interrupt-probe.mjs` and `scripts/codex-interrupt-probe.mjs` confirmed the protocol at CLI level (`artifacts/*-interrupt-probe.json`). `scripts/persistent-native-smoke.mjs` then drove the isolated `scripts/native-persistent.tauri.json` app on CDP 9517 through real chats: for each provider the second reply reused the first reply's process ID, Stop during a streamed long answer left that process alive and a later reply reused it, and a message queued during a running reply was sent automatically afterwards on the same process (`artifacts/persistent/native-report.json`, `queued-{claude,codex}.png`). Closing the QA window released both parked CLI processes. The first native run exposed and fixed a real defect: a parked Codex process carried the app's pre-bind session ID, so the second reply restarted instead of reusing until the process records the provider-reported thread ID. Stopped replies keep streamed partial text only in progress blocks, as before. WSL, macOS/Linux, separate login profiles, remote Viewer runs, and Antigravity were not live-tested; Antigravity keeps its one-process-per-reply path.

To repeat: build the frontend, then in PowerShell set `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER` to `artifacts/native-persistent-webview` and `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS` to `--remote-debugging-port=9517`, run `npm run tauri dev -- --no-watch --no-dev-server --config scripts/native-persistent.tauri.json`, and run `node scripts/persistent-native-smoke.mjs`. The script rejects a QA workspace with unrelated conversation titles and never targets the regular app identifier.

An isolated Windows WebView2 app (`com.vinicius.agentstudio.scroll-qa`) also reached scrollTop 0 and the full 4404px bottom with native mouse-wheel input, using a saved fixture with 80 answer paragraphs and 30 tool cards. The composer stayed inside the viewport and no runtime exceptions were reported. Streaming events are controlled browser fixtures; the native check covers rendered history and input behavior, not a new provider integration. Evidence is in `artifacts/chat-scroll-before-*.png`, `chat-scroll-{desktop,mobile}.png`, `chat-scroll-native*.png`, and `chat-scroll-native-result.json`.

## Single admin workspace — 2026-09-12

The private registry now enforces exactly one enabled administrator. Creating workspaces always creates members; transferring administration to an existing enabled workspace atomically demotes the previous admin. Direct removal or disablement of the only admin and stale/concurrent former-admin mutations are rejected. Version 1 upgrades preserve the owner when already admin, otherwise the oldest enabled admin, and retain keys, sessions, and chat files. Invalid version 2 registries fail closed. Rollback to an older release requires restoring only the pre-upgrade registry while stopped, preserving current chats and sessions.

Nineteen administration HTTP/CLI cases cover the new invariant, migration, member-only creation, disabled targets, concurrency, revocation, and corruption. Together with seventeen private-workspace cases, all 36 focused tests passed. Four built-PWA scenarios passed desktop and 390px layouts, transfer in both directions, immediate removal of former-admin controls, member-only creation, transient key disclosure, and access changes during pending requests. The transfer-success assertion targets its specific status text because the sidebar badge also has a status role.

The real Windows smoke passed through the rendered Tauri UI using `scripts/native-single-admin.tauri.json`, the isolated `artifacts/single-admin-native-target`, a separate WebView2 profile, and CDP 9521. Set `AGENT_STUDIO_ADMIN_QA_PORT=9521`, `AGENT_STUDIO_ADMIN_QA_IDENTIFIER=com.vinicius.agentstudio.single-admin-qa`, and `AGENT_STUDIO_ADMIN_QA_OUTPUT=artifacts/single-admin-native` before running `node scripts/workspace-admin-native-smoke.mjs`. It created a member, transferred administration through the UI, verified exactly one admin, rejected a former-admin mutation, preserved chats, and kept the issued key out of saved data. Evidence: `artifacts/single-admin-native/{result.json,connections.png}`; the screenshot was inspected and there were no renderer errors. Existing apps and CLI logins were preserved. Provider prompts were unnecessary; macOS/Linux native and physical phones were not repeated.

Final gates passed: `npm run verify` reported zero Svelte errors/warnings, 156 unit tests, a production build, and all 96 browser tests. Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 86 Rust tests passed (four existing opt-in tests ignored). Logs use `artifacts/single-admin-{verify,fmt,clippy,rust}.log`; desktop and phone administration screenshots under `test-results/workspace-admin-*` were inspected. The release process includes a private pre-upgrade backup and registry-aware rollback, checks every workspace checkpoint for active replies before restart, and verifies retained chat/message identities and active session digests over the upgraded service without disclosing keys or chat contents.

## Admin workspaces — 2026-09-11

The original owner workspace defaults to Administrator and additional workspaces default to Member. Connections provides workspace creation, naming, role assignment, key rotation, and disablement. ## Pending chat badges and foreground completion alerts — 2026-09-11

A shared count includes all Active chats without a running assistant reply, independently of the selected chat, History tab, or search query. It drives the sidebar's numbered badge and device-local app-icon updates through transport. Windows renders an application-owned taskbar overlay (99+ above 99), macOS/Linux use native count APIs, and supported browsers use `setAppBadge`/`clearAppBadge`. Relay delivery computes the current count for each push attempt, including a matching terminal job before its final workspace checkpoint; workers apply the exact count without requiring a page or incrementing per notification. Closed PWAs update on a lifecycle push or their next open/sync, rather than receiving extra visible alerts for archive-only changes.

Focused browser coverage proves idle/running counts, retained drafts and notifications when another chat completes, unchanged totals during reading/search, archive/delete decrements, and clearing at zero. A real compiled worker receives a push with no app page open and calls the browser badge API with the relay total, then clears it on zero. Unit coverage adds relay-result reconciliation, terminal/history exclusions, unsupported browser handling, and current counts on retries. Native Rust coverage verifies transparent overlay pixels, distinct digits, zero clearing, and 99+ bounds. ## Model context MCPs and scoped memories — 2026-09-11

Model context now includes an MCPs category with bounded server names and status/scope metadata. Codex starts its metadata app-server in the selected folder, reads effective configuration, and follows bounded MCP status pagination. Claude retains MCP discovery during its tool-disabled, hook-disabled context query and reads native MCP status. No model replies, tool calls, or sign-in flows are requested. Configured and connected are distinct; disabled, failed, connecting, and authentication-required states remain visible. Commands, URLs, environment values, tool definitions, and raw errors are excluded from returned metadata.

Codex memory discovery now lists the selected profile's global summary/index plus existing registry references whose workspace belongs to the current project/folder. ## Active deletion and authentication before sending — 2026-09-11

Active conversations can now be deleted during a response. Confirmation cancels the exact local or remote run and waits for completion before removal; cancellation failures retain the conversation and allow retry. Local storage failures also retain the chat. Remote deletion merges the latest checkpoint, and three-way sync lets deletion win over delayed reply/generated-title updates while preserving deliberate edits as conflict copies. This supersedes the earlier running-conversation restriction below.

Send and Retry require verified authentication for the selected connection. Signed-out or unknown accounts show a warning with Open Connections; blocked submissions preserve drafts, images, and History state. Automatic model queries wait for ready authentication, and native catalogs launch only the requested provider. Gemini performs the existing headless account query before chat or model listing and terminates authentication-required output promptly. [Antigravity's headless documentation](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/) describes cached-login requirements for noninteractive runs.

The final gate passed `npm run verify` (zero diagnostics, 80 unit/HTTP tests, production build, 71 browser scenarios), Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 65 Rust tests with four pre-existing opt-in integrations ignored. Coverage includes active/current/other-chat deletion, cancellation failure/retry, late events and sync checkpoints, signed-out/unknown submission and retry guards, account recovery, retained images/drafts/History, cold detection, and mobile warning bounds. Three older scenarios were updated to wait for authentication before model requests or retry. Logs: `artifacts/active-auth-{verify,rust}-final.log`.

An isolated `npm run tauri dev` app (`com.vinicius.agentstudio.active-auth-qa`, separate Cargo target/WebView profile, CDP 9482) verified real Antigravity 1.2.1 authentication and a completed Gemini reply, then right-clicked and deleted its next running response. ## Windows desktop pairing and automatic reconnect — 2026-09-10

The user's PWA correctly remained Offline after the desktop reopened in **Local only** mode: desktop relay authentication was intentionally memory-only. With the user's approval, Windows now saves its own relay origin/key in Windows Credential Manager, scoped to the current Windows user, app identifier, and installation. Native restoration returns only the origin to the renderer. Startup retries temporary failures, including while minimized; explicit disconnect removes the saved pairing and cannot be undone by an older restoration. Other native platforms keep temporary pairing. No provider credential behavior changed.

The real Windows credential test covers storing, loading, replacing, isolating, refusing oversized replacements, and deleting a synthetic pairing. Transport regressions cover restoration/heartbeat, absent pairing, temporary failure, replaced-server rejection, late restoration after disconnect, and failed deletion. `scripts/relay-reconnect-native-smoke.mjs` used `npm run tauri dev -- --no-watch --config scripts/native-relay.tauri.json`, an isolated app identity/WebView profile and CDP 9473, Vite 1435, and a real HTTP relay. It closed and relaunched the actual executable, held the server unavailable during startup, then observed the same 390×844 PWA page change from Offline to Online without reloading. App/browser identities were preserved; the key was absent from native workspace/checkpoint files and renderer storage. Disconnect remained effective after another process restart. No renderer errors occurred. Screenshots were visually reviewed under `artifacts/relay-reconnect-native/`.

The first complete browser gate exposed a previously unpersisted empty relay identity. A normal restart before any workspace edits generated a different instance ID and tripped the new replacement guard. The relay now atomically saves its initial empty workspace/identity before accepting clients; a real HTTP regression and the existing service-worker/server-restart test pass. Unreadable state is still preserved and rejected.

Final full gate passed: `npm run verify -- -- --config artifacts/reconnect.playwright.config.ts` (only the isolated Vite origin/test-output paths differ from the standard config), zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 77 unit/HTTP tests, production build, and all 65 browser scenarios. Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 64 Rust tests passed; four pre-existing opt-in integrations remain ignored. Logs are `artifacts/relay-reconnect-{verify,clippy,rust}.log`. Native restart/PWA recovery was exercised without sending provider prompts. Physical-phone behavior and new provider replies were not retested. Existing desktops need one pairing after upgrading from the memory-only version.

## PWA pairing across updates — 2026-09-10

- Browser sessions previously lived only in the relay's memory, so a deployment restart discarded every phone's pairing. Session-ID digests now persist separately from workspace/public files, with atomic restricted writes and pairing-key binding. Coverage verifies restart/reload, daily renewal with retained Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite flags, inactivity expiry, durable logout, key rotation including switching back, unreadable-file preservation, failed writes, and rejection of a digest used as a cookie. Older memory-only sessions require one final pairing when first upgrading.
- The PWA automatically retries unavailable session/state endpoints after startup and renews its session while syncing. The regression actually restarts the HTTP server on the same origin/data directory, installs and activates an updated service worker, reopens with the same cookie/device identity, and recovers from server-side 503s without focus/reload/pairing actions. Explicit disconnect and API cache exclusion remain verified. No commands are replayed.
- Final local gate: `npm run verify` passed with zero diagnostics, 71 unit/HTTP tests, production build, and 65 browser scenarios. Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 63 tests passed (four existing opt-in integrations ignored). The Linux container built and passed its public-shell/pairing/API-boundary smoke. Both mobile HTTP scenarios passed in Windows WebKit. Chromium and WebKit reconnect screenshots were visually reviewed. Evidence: `artifacts/pwa-session-verify.log`, `artifacts/pwa-session-rust-*.log`, `artifacts/pwa-session/`, and `test-results/mobile-pairing-survives-a--ad39c-tries-an-unavailable-server/`.
- Harness corrections: restart fixtures send `Connection: close` to avoid Node reusing a dead pooled socket (`ECONNRESET`); eight session tests passed five consecutive repeats after this correction. WebKit outages use actual server responses rather than service-worker request interception. Windows WebKit reports Strict cookies as None, an [upstream expected failure](https://github.com/microsoft/playwright/blob/main/tests/library/browsercontext-cookies.spec.ts); the response header is asserted on every engine and stored SameSite remains asserted on supported engines. Stopping the real server verifies cached-shell loading where WinCairo offline emulation raises an internal navigation error. These are browser checks; physical iPhone/Android update behavior and new native provider replies were not retested.

## iOS 26.5.2 footer clipping and page bounds — 2026-09-10

- The user confirmed that the previous bottom-gap patch did not resolve the gap and clipped the usage tracks with the keyboard closed. That report supersedes the simulated success described below. The maximum-height workaround enlarged only the fixed shell while leaving a percentage-height root/body, and could exceed the drawable page. Removed that workaround, sized the root/body and shell with `100dvh`, bounded the main grid row, and replaced the translucent status-bar request with an opaque black status bar. The top safe-area padding now subtracts any visual viewport pan; the drawer, like the composer, removes its bottom inset while the keyboard is open.
- The replacement regression keeps an actual 844px browser viewport while reporting a 906px fullscreen height. Before the fix it failed with an app bottom of 906px. It supplies nonzero 59px top and 34px bottom insets, checks root/body/shell/composer/drawer/footer bounds, and hit-tests both vertical edges of every 6px usage track at rest, after keyboard panning, after dismissal, and after resizing. This replaces the earlier fixture that artificially shortened only the CSS fallback and checked outer boxes. Chromium and Windows WebKit both pass the focused scenarios; the WebKit safe-area chat/drawer screenshots were visually reviewed. Evidence is under `artifacts/mobile-insets/` and `artifacts/mobile-layout/webkit-results/`.
- These are browser regressions, not a physical iPhone verification. [WebKit 301994 comment 12](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=301994#c12) reports a system-owned gap on this exact iOS version; matching symptoms are evidence for investigation, not proof that this phone has that engine bug. App sizing cannot paint into an area owned by iOS. Device confirmation after loading the new app remains necessary, including whether the changed status-bar mode is honored by the existing home-screen installation.

## Automatic discovery and additional-account sign-in — 2026-09-09

- This replaces the preceding account-picker behavior recorded below. A named event confirms shell readiness; process-exit checks expose startup failures. The shell retains command errors with `-NoExit`. This follows Microsoft's [console creation semantics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/console/creation-of-a-console).

## Selected computer controls execution — 2026-09-09

- This policy supersedes the historical Windows-first entries below. Desktop uses only its native CLI, including when its folder is in a managed WSL distribution. Selecting a WSL computer uses only that distribution's Linux CLI for existing logins and separate profiles. Missing executables and failed operations never select another computer or account.
- Folder ownership remains stable. An additive `executionEnvironmentId` records Desktop execution for a WSL folder; omitted values retain the folder's environment. Connection validation rejects other computers and sibling distributions. Recent folders, history, sidebar actions, context cache keys, and relay snapshots preserve both identities. Starting chat from a computer card explicitly selects that computer instead of inheriting a recent location on another one.
- Each WSL distribution has its own computer card and installation inventory. Missing CLIs show installation guidance and cannot connect/sign in; failed inventory checks remain unknown or stale. Native executable lookup and Linux PATH inventory are independent of authentication and never read credentials.
- Automated coverage includes restored Desktop/WSL choices for the same Linux folder, Linux-only provider choices, missing installation actions, direct computer-card navigation, invalid location/connection combinations, scoped context caching, and preservation through sync. Rust formatting and Clippy with warnings denied passed, as did all 60 default Rust tests. Both opt-in process fixtures passed: a real Windows PowerShell process opened the selected WSL directory, and the real WSL bridge preserved stdin, profile isolation, Linux cwd, and owned descendant cancellation.
- Final `npm run verify` passed: zero Svelte/TypeScript errors or warnings, 59 frontend/relay unit tests, production build, and all 49 browser scenarios. Log: `artifacts/selected-computer-verify.log`. The final native smoke passed at **2026-09-09T18:20:05Z**. A test initially expected the wrong empty-folder label (`Choose folder`); it was corrected to the existing `Browse folders…` label, and the focused regression plus complete verification then passed.
- Native `npm run tauri dev` QA used a separate Cargo target, app identifier, and WebView profile on CDP 9440. `scripts/wsl-computers-native-smoke.mjs` verified real Windows Codex detection for a WSL folder and missing Ubuntu Codex without fallback; missing Linux CLIs disabled Add account and Send and hid Connect account. The script restored its isolated workspace, sent no provider prompts, and recorded no renderer errors. ## Windows Claude first for WSL folders

- Fixed the reported `Claude needs to be set up` warning for a WSL folder when Claude is installed on Windows. The connection resolver previously returned the selected WSL distribution before checking for the native executable. Existing WSL Codex/Claude logins now resolve Windows first and fall back only to that distribution when the Windows executable is missing. All detection, login, usage, title, model-query, and response calls share this resolver. Separate WSL account profiles remain scoped to their own login; authentication or response errors do not replay on another account.
- The folder and stored connection identity stay unchanged. Before a Windows response, a fixed WSL script receives the Linux folder as a positional argument, validates it by changing directory, and returns its Windows/UNC equivalent. Windows starts in that directory; the Linux fallback retains the original Linux cwd. No project files or credentials are generated/copied. Missing folders and names Windows cannot represent fail before the provider starts. Directory resolution is cancellable before launch.

## Responsive folder and model selection

- Removed the serial all-connection CLI checks and relay sync from folder selection. Folder acceptance validates the path, persists the selection, and lets missing connection checks finish in the background. Known availability is reused for folders in the same environment; concurrent detection requests are deduplicated. Reselecting the current computer or folder preserves the folder, draft, and settings without another lookup.
- Model catalogs are cached for five minutes per provider and connection configuration. Cached/default and saved model choices remain selectable during requests, including manual refresh. Native queries wait for newly created connections to be saved. Late detection/catalog replies cannot change newer selections, and unknown local availability never enables sending. Offline remote routing remains pinned.

## Sign-in launcher correction

The initial direct CLI launch closed immediately without a persistent interactive shell. Routing PowerShell through an existing Windows Terminal process then produced `-File ... does not exist` and `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, even though those files were accessible to the app and command tools.

The launcher owns a dedicated `conhost.exe` process, launches PowerShell with `-NoExit -EncodedCommand`, and quotes every executable/path argument as a literal. This removes the temporary-script dependency and the existing-terminal handoff.

## Gemini subscription migration

The user's subsequent screenshot showed browser authentication succeeded, followed by “This client is no longer supported for Gemini Code Assist for individuals.” The original login-needed diagnosis was incomplete: Google [retired individual Gemini CLI access on June 18, 2026](https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli/discussions/28017). Surviving console processes did not establish usable authentication.

Installed Antigravity CLI 1.1.27 using Google's installer with its SHA-512 check. The user completed its Google sign-in. The Gemini adapter now resolves `agy`, including `%LOCALAPPDATA%/agy/bin` before a PATH refresh, uses its NDJSON input and output protocol, and never falls back to the retired CLI. Workspace version and provider IDs are unchanged, preserving existing data.

Live packaged-app evidence: `artifacts/gemini-release-smoke.json` and `gemini-release-chat.png`. Gemini replied “Noted.”, then correctly recalled “maple” in a follow-up; the Connections badge changed to Connected. Synthetic conversations from this check are removed using a unique run marker; the user's other conversations are preserved.

Tool restrictions required a runtime check. An empty primary-agent tool list did not remove the advertised tool set. More importantly, `current_dir` alone did not load workspace hooks: `agy -p /hooks` returned an empty list, and a benign test write succeeded. Adding `--add-dir` for the app-owned directory loaded the hook. With the wildcard `PreToolUse` denial active, a real `write_to_file` attempt was blocked and its target remained absent. The opt-in `scripts/antigravity-permissions-smoke.mjs` repeats this check against the hook generated by the running app. No global credentials or permission settings are edited.

For WebView2 QA, an existing profile coincided with a blank/unresponsive startup during this session. A separate `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER` allowed intermediate packaged-app verification. The final executable was then restarted with the normal profile and passed the real Gemini reply and follow-up checks again at 14:17 UTC. Authentication remains in the external CLI and was unaffected. The temporary profile workaround is not evidence of a general production startup fix.

## Sign-in status correction

The migrated adapter could chat, but detection still returned `unknown` for Gemini. Only a completed chat set `ready`, and Refresh or restart reverted that status. This left Connections showing Installed after a successful CLI login. The previous real-chat smoke did not cover this acceptance condition.

Detection now runs `agy --print /usage --output-format json` with closed stdin and a 20-second timeout. A live CLI probe returned `status: SUCCESS`, `command: usage`, zero turns, and zero input/output model tokens. The app requires both a successful process exit and that specific response before showing Connected. It stops at the headless authentication-required message and distinguishes an unverified connection from a signed-out account. Raw account output and credentials are not persisted or displayed.

The UI refreshes on startup, window focus, and visibility changes. After Open sign-in it polls every five seconds for up to ten minutes, so it does not depend on the interactive CLI exiting. Browser regressions verify that launching a console alone does not mark an account connected, and that completing sign-in updates the badge without a chat.

Opt-in native check: `node scripts/login-status-smoke.mjs` with the packaged app on the QA CDP port. It verifies Connected before a chat, after Refresh, after reload, and after focus, while asserting the saved workspace is unchanged.

The rebuilt release passed this check with the normal WebView2 profile at 14:31 UTC: Gemini showed Connected before sending any chat, and remained Connected after manual Refresh, reload, and focus. The workspace was unchanged and no page errors occurred. Evidence: `artifacts/login-status-smoke.json` and `artifacts/login-status-connections.png`. Signed-out behavior was covered by parser and browser fixtures; the user's live CLI account was not logged out for testing.

## Chat configuration replaces preset agents

The separate agent library is removed. Every conversation has Agent, Model, and Reasoning selectors in the chat header, editable between replies. Latest model is stored per provider; reasoning is stored independently per provider/model, including the Default selection. Reopening a chat preserves its own settings. Each reply carries its generating configuration, so changing providers does not relabel historical messages. Optional instructions are scoped to a chat.

Codex model metadata comes from its installed app-server `model/list`. Gemini model variants from `agy models` are grouped by base model and supported effort. Claude choices use its documented aliases and supported effort values; its account/organization policy can still restrict availability or cap effort. References: [Codex model listing](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app-server#list-models-modellist), [Claude model and effort configuration](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config), [Antigravity model and effort selection](https://www.antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/).

Workspace v2 migration preserves all existing chat IDs, messages, instructions, and authors. Retired presets are retained in `legacyAgents`; the first desktop save also backs up the exact v1 file to `workspace-v1-backup.json`. Browser preview remains on its existing storage key so prior preview data can migrate too.

The packaged app passed `scripts/chat-settings-smoke.mjs` at 15:00 UTC. One synthetic conversation received real replies from Codex / GPT-5.6 Sol / Low, Claude / Sonnet / Low, and Gemini / 3.8 Flash / High. Both provider switches retained the earlier context. Configurations reached the CLI, were saved with each response, and the latest choices survived new-chat creation and reload. The original chats were unchanged after migration, the v1 backup matched the original bytes, and the test restored the user's preferences and removed its unique synthetic chat. Evidence: `artifacts/chat-settings-smoke.json`, `chat-settings-native.png`, `chat-settings-browser.png`, and `chat-settings-compact.png`. No page errors were observed. Other model/effort combinations were not exhaustively tested against live accounts.

The final package also passed a read-only startup/model-picker/reload check after the model-error wording was updated to point to the chat header. Evidence: `artifacts/chat-settings-final-check.json` and `chat-settings-final.png`. It was reopened normally without a debugging listener.

## Automatic titles

Each new conversation makes one background title request from the first 2,000 characters of its first message. Fixed small-model choices are Codex GPT-5.6 Luna / Low, Claude Haiku, and Gemini 3.8 Flash / Low. The Rust adapter retains all chat tool restrictions and uses a separate runtime directory, cancellation registry, 30-second deadline, and bounded output. Failure preserves the first-message excerpt. Restarts do not retry unfinished title requests; existing conversations are not renamed. Deleting a chat cancels its title job, and a late result cannot recreate it. Window close also cancels title processes.

Browser regressions verify the chat can finish before its title, generated names survive reload, provider/model/reasoning preferences stay unchanged, follow-up messages do not generate another title, failures preserve working chats, and late results remain tied to the original conversation. Domain restoration converts interrupted title jobs to fallback. Rust checks validate fixed model selection, Unicode-safe context limits, and short single-line output.

Build-order finding: do not run browser checks against the reused Vite server while the Tauri frontend build rewrites `.svelte-kit`; that triggers unexpected full-page reloads in tests. The unchanged browser suite passed after serializing those operations. The durable finding was also recorded in the local memory notes.

The packaged release passed `scripts/titles-smoke.mjs` at 15:32 UTC. A real Codex/Sol conversation was named **Balcony Herb Garden Planning** by Luna in parallel with its reply, retained its Sol/Low settings, and kept the title after reload. Direct native title requests also succeeded with Claude Haiku and Gemini 3.8 Flash/Low, both returning Portuguese titles from a Portuguese first message. Original conversations were unchanged, preferences were restored, and the unique synthetic chat was removed. Evidence: `artifacts/titles-smoke.json`, `titles-native.png`, and `titles-verify.log`. All 36 tests passed; no native page errors occurred. The screenshot was taken just after reload, before provider detection completed, so its transient setup label is not an authentication result.

No API keys, token-file access, or model generation are used for quota refreshes. See [data sources and context semantics](USAGE.md).

The app keeps these omissions and model scopes intact. Fable's card disappeared when switching to Sonnet.

Browser tests cover missing quotas, refresh failure with preserved readings, provider-switch races, model-specific capacity, draft growth, and reload persistence. Context occupancy is explicitly an estimate of the next replayed request, not an exact tokenizer reading or a sum of historical billed tokens. The first reply calibrates otherwise-unmeasured CLI overhead. Unknown capacity and unmeasured older replies are labelled accordingly.

## Reported context correction

The initial usage implementation placed a next-request estimate in the primary context meter. After the user asked for Claude's actual count, the main meter now uses the last compatible response's `contextInput` and reported window. It no longer requires a saved local prompt estimate, changes while typing, or substitutes cumulative billing totals. The response's measured window takes priority over later catalog data. Changing provider/model/reasoning/instructions requires a matching new measurement. A pending reply keeps the previous reading; a later completed reply without context data clears it. An explicit zero is preserved.

Claude's request count includes uncached, cache-read, and cache-creation input once, matching its documented status-line semantics. The parser clears its previous request count if the latest assistant message omits usage. The current Codex exec and Antigravity result adapters still expose aggregate totals; their main context meter says **Not measured yet**. This is a current adapter boundary, not a claim that those providers cannot expose context. A separate labelled next-request forecast includes the draft and remains available for all providers.

All 56 automated tests passed: 13 Vitest, 20 browser, and 23 Rust. Browser checks assert exact percentages, draft independence, model-change invalidation, pending and missing readings, saved token persistence, and no cache double-counting. Svelte/TypeScript, static build, formatting, and Clippy also passed. Evidence: `artifacts/context-verify.log`, `context-reported-browser.png`, and `context-reported-compact.png`. The earlier usage section records historical verification of the estimate-based UI; this section describes the current behavior.

The rebuilt packaged app passed live verification at 16:34 UTC. Claude/Haiku reported 3,665 input tokens and a 200,000-token window; the main meter matched that ratio exactly, stayed fixed while a draft changed the separate estimate, and retained the same count after reload. Codex/Luna and Gemini/3.8 Flash both completed real replies and retained their token totals while their unmeasured main context stayed unknown. Live subscription readings and Fable-only visibility still passed. The final read-only check verified that a new Fable chat stays unmeasured even while a draft forecast grows. No native page errors occurred; original chats and preferences were preserved and all synthetic chats removed. Evidence: `artifacts/context-smoke.json`, `context-{claude,codex,gemini}-native.png`, and `usage-final-check.json`. The normal app was reopened after QA with no debugging listener. The context-display correction was recorded in local memory notes.

## Quota pace and context guidance

Quota pace compares used percentage with the elapsed share of the reported window, aligned to the reading's timestamp. A five-percentage-point tolerance defines **On track**; lower readings show **Below pace**, higher readings **Ahead of pace**, and 100% **Limit reached**. Guidance includes a marker on the usage bar, projected use at reset after 5% of the window has elapsed, remaining hourly/daily allowance, and advice to ease usage when ahead. Stale, failed, missing, expired, and inconsistent readings withhold the pace classification. The guide explicitly assumes evenly spread use over the full window.

Context guidance uses actual measured room and up to five consecutive growth increments, requiring three compatible readings. It stops at missing data, changed settings/model/window, or a decrease. Room/watch/near-full/full thresholds use measured percentages; fast growth warns when about three or fewer similar exchanges would reach 80%. Draft estimates have separate warnings and never move measured context or its status. These calculations use existing readings and saved replies without extra provider queries.

All 65 automated tests passed, along with Svelte/TypeScript, static build, Rust formatting, and Clippy. The browser suite checks all three pace labels, reduction/allowance text, Fable scope, withdrawal of guidance on refresh failure, responsive layout, context growth after reload, and independent draft warnings. Evidence: `artifacts/pace-verify.log`, `pace-browser.png`, and `pace-compact.png`. Source semantics and examples are documented in [USAGE.md](USAGE.md).

The final packaged release passed live checks at 17:39 UTC. Its independently calculated Claude five-hour and weekly labels were On track, while Fable's separate weekly quota was Ahead of pace. Three Haiku replies reported 3,668, 3,728, and 3,788 context tokens, producing a measured growth trend of 60 tokens per exchange. The exact context ratio and guidance stayed fixed while typing and survived reload. Distant projections use qualitative wording beyond 20 exchanges instead of implying thousands of available turns. The original workspace and preferences were preserved, synthetic chats removed, and no page errors occurred. Evidence: `artifacts/pace-smoke.json`, `pace-quotas-native.png`, and `pace-context-native.png`.

Native QA now waits for packaged-page navigation after CDP connection; attaching during `about:blank` previously caused `Packaged app not found`. The startup race and guidance semantics were recorded in the user's local memory notes. The rebuilt executable and NSIS installer are recorded in `artifacts/build-manifest.json`. The final app was reopened with its normal profile, responded normally, and had no QA debugging listener.

## Boundaries

- Saved workflow execution, native CLI session resumption, file attachments, and executable HTML artifacts are future work. Claude and Codex chat tools are enabled as described below.
- The web build is an honest UI preview; a future browser version needs an authenticated process backend.
- Windows was tested. macOS and Linux were not; use their terminal sign-in commands.
- The installer is unsigned. Building it does not establish successful installation or uninstallation on another machine.
- A real provider quota/timeout condition was not deliberately induced. Provider error-event fixtures were tested.
- One response runs at a time. Full conversation replay is bounded to 200 messages / 400 KB and may use more tokens than CLI session resumption.

Rerunning the smoke reuses its saved reply instead of generating another one.

The existing app and its chats were left running; its older native process requires a rebuilt/restarted app to capture costs on future replies. The separate cost QA app contains the updated native adapter. No installer was rebuilt. Codex's generated `ThreadTokenUsage`/`TokenUsageBreakdown` contract was inspected and has no monetary field; no new Codex or Gemini provider reply was needed for this cost-only adapter addition.

## Cumulative AI reply time — 2026-09-09

Each reply's usage row now includes total AI time through that reply, with the same value in the expanded breakdown. A single pass over the selected conversation sums recorded completed, failed, and stopped assistant reply durations. User messages, running replies, and other conversations are excluded. Missing historical timing produces a lower-bound total with an explanation; all-missing timing remains unknown. Totals use unrounded milliseconds before formatting with minutes/hours and are derived from saved messages rather than persisting another counter. Retried messages that the existing retry action removes are no longer part of saved history.

All required checks passed: `npm run verify` with zero diagnostics, 57 frontend/HTTP unit tests, production build, and 47 browser tests; Rust formatting, Clippy with warnings denied, and 58 Rust tests passed (three existing opt-in tests ignored). New coverage checks cumulative values across model/status changes, missing/zero timing, formatting boundaries, conversation isolation, live completion updates, unchanged earlier totals, and reopening saved history.

Native verification reused the existing saved Claude reply in the isolated cost QA app on port 9458, generated no new provider replies, and confirmed its 4583.3ms stored duration displays as both 4.6s elapsed and 4.6s total. Summary/expanded values and no overflow at 1380px and 880px were checked. Evidence: `artifacts/reply-time-native-result.json`, `reply-time-wide-native.png`, `reply-time-narrow-native.png`, and browser fixture `reply-time-total-narrow-browser.png`; the narrow native layout and multi-reply browser state were visually reviewed. No native adapter or storage schema changes were needed; both existing app processes were left running.

Documentation-edit note: PowerShell treats typographic apostrophes as quote delimiters inside single-quoted strings. An inline replacement containing one failed before writing with `Missing ')' in method call.` Rewording the replacement without the embedded apostrophe succeeded and the resulting README sentence was verified with `rg`. Use `apply_patch` or a literal here-string for prose containing apostrophes.

## Redesigned folder browser — 2026-09-22

The **Choose a folder** dialog is now a wide, fixed-height browser whose list scrolls internally. It opens at the currently selected folder of the same environment (otherwise Home), shows the path as clickable breadcrumbs with an **Edit path** mode (Ctrl+L), and adds a **Quick access** column with Recent folders for the selected computer and environment, Home, existing common project folders, Windows drive roots or WSL drive mounts, and the Linux root. The filter box narrows the open folder (prefix matches first), opens the first match on Enter, and jumps to pasted absolute or `~/` paths. Hidden folders (dot-prefixed names or Windows hidden/system attributes) stay hidden until **Show hidden** is on, which is remembered per device; folders containing `.git` carry a repository marker. Each row offers **Use** to select it without opening, Arrow/Enter/Backspace navigate the list, and a failed path keeps its breadcrumbs with the error while **Use this folder** is disabled. Narrow windows show Quick access as a chip strip. The host still returns directory names only: hidden and repository flags come from names, attributes, and `.git` presence, and the WSL script now emits `@kind:path` places and `flags:name` records. Places are optional in listings so older relay hosts keep working, and the toolbar Folder dropdown keeps its environment detail with hover-only paths.

All required checks passed on the final code: `npm run verify` (zero Svelte/TypeScript diagnostics, 265 frontend/HTTP tests in 45 files, production build, 189 browser scenarios including the new `tests/folder-browser.spec.ts`), `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy with warnings denied, and 239 Rust tests with six existing opt-in integrations ignored. New Rust tests cover hidden/repository flags, `.git` worktree files, the extended WSL record parser, its 1,000-entry truncation, and quick-access places; `folders.test.ts` covers breadcrumb segmentation for Windows, POSIX, and UNC paths, absolute-path detection, home expansion, path compaction, and filtering. The two existing browse scenarios now open **Edit path** before typing a path. Reviewed `artifacts/folder-browser-desktop.png` (1380×900) and `artifacts/folder-browser-mobile.png` (390×844). Native WSL folder browsing was not rerun against a real distribution in this session; the embedded script change is covered by the parser tests only.
